## Summary

This GDD defines how eldritch exposure (depth, Attention events and real horror contact) lowers each player's hidden sanity level, and how that level rewrites perception as falsifiable misinformation rather than a stat penalty. In the MVP that is class (b), false system states on gauges, inspect cards, glyphs, lamp-housing glow sprites and machine sprites, each of which always leaks, never touches the consequence channel and can always be checked at a priced cost on a four-rung verification ladder (the room, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, time); phantom monsters, crewmates drawn as horrors with system-gated lethal friendly harm, and phantom salvage are designed to the same depth behind switches. Read it if you build the Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio, Interaction's harm gate or Save/Load for Sanity, or if you tune pacing and fairness: it carries the lead-time invariants, the ambient floor, the acceptance criteria (counted in Acceptance Criteria section C) and the open questions. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the count is stated once, in section C.)*

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed), Attention System (designed), Interaction System (designed), Suit & Equipment (designed), Save/Load & Versioned Format (designed); soft: Life-Support Graph, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Ship Section & Layout (all designed); Horror Entities (Designed, horror-entities.md), Web Map & Transit (Designed, web-map-and-transit.md); Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, Boarding & Salvage (all designed)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Overview

Sanity & Hallucination Rules is the ship's legibility turned against its keeper, as simulation. In one sentence: it keeps one hidden integer per seat, the **sanity level**, that the web's regard and the crew's depth wear down and that rest in a settled home compartment restores, and it turns that level into a small number of **rules for what the player's own senses may be made to say falsely**, always about a surface the world can contradict, always at a price the player can see before paying it. It is a scene-tree-free, integer-only system (H10) with one divisor-12 step hook, `sanity.drift`, three registered state blocks (`sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`, `sanity.perception`), two Class B mutators that Interaction calls (`record_falsification`, `record_harm_event`) and one SIM query Interaction's `validate` calls (`actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`). It submits no command and registers no player input.

**The data layer.** Per seat (keyed by PlayerId, the stable seat identity, never a transport peer ID) the system holds a level in milli-sanity-units (`mSU`, `SAN_MAX` 100000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`), the derived **stage** 0 to 3 (0 sane; 1 the instruments; 2 the light; 3 the world's hue, ART 4.4), two orthogonal flags (`tell_on`, the victim-tell bit; `harm_eligible`, the friendly-harm gate, one SIM bit), and a **lie ledger**: the live record of which module-body surface is lying to this seat, how, until when, and what leak it carries. The level moves in one place only (formula D.1): exposure from a closed list of true, simulation-owned inputs (depth, being aboard a foreign body, Attention's phase and tier, real horror contact, and in the dormant co-op slice a witnessed crew death) minus recovery (a settled home compartment, and in the dormant slice a saner crewmate), under a net cap per run so that lethal friendly harm cannot unlock inside minutes. The ledger is Sanity's own SIM state because `PERCEPTION_OUT` is write-only to simulation, including to its own owner's hook (CR-12); the PERCEPTION_OUT block, `sanity.perception`, is a **pure per-seat projection** of the ledger that only the Perception Filter reads. Four hallucination classes are specified: **(a)** phantom entities and hidden real ones, **(b)** false system states, **(c)** crewmates drawn as horrors, with lethal friendly harm system-gated on the *attacker's* level, and **(d)** phantom salvage. Every class passes the same eligibility guard rails (SN-15): the lie targets only the corruptible module-body channel, keeps a live witness on the consequence channel, leaves a second instrument standing, always carries its leak, and grants uniform immunity after a check. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the rails are applied by the lie's effective **direction** (comfort, alarm or neutral), not by its kind name; a comfort-direction lie of any kind (FALSE_OK, GLIDE toward health, a frame swap to the healthier frame) takes the severity ceiling, the atmosphere guard and the active-witness requirement, SN-15 L-D.)*

**The player-facing effect.** The player never sees a number, a bar, a ring, a vignette or a word for the state; the game has no noun for it. They meet it as a gauge they have read a hundred times whose needle will not rest and whose glass edge carries a faint violet gleam; as a duct panel reading green while the dust in their beam skates toward an open hatch and a hiss comes through its frame *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the disagreement the MVP stages is gauge versus the room's own witnesses; the Suit's breath cue arrives when the seat steps into the leaking room and is the moment a comfort lie ends, never a witness that coexists with it, SN-15 L-G)*; as a pump sprite spinning on a dead branch; later *(VS-A)* as a lamp cone that seems to reach farther than it does, with a fringe at its rim; later still as the world's hue bleeding violet at pipe edges, a thing in the corner of the beam that has no footprint *(VS-A)*, and, for an actor already deep, a crewmate drawn as a horror who still walks like a technician, holds a true Tungsten beam and speaks in a true voice *(VS-B)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the MVP's stage-2 beat is the own-cone rim fringe (lie-bound: drawn only while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE; user ruling OQ-SN-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* plus the state-glyph and lamp-housing glow-sprite lies; a cone that reaches farther than it does is a Lighting decoy kind and ships at VS-A, Visual/Audio preamble item 1.)* They answer by **asking the world** and paying the price: walk to the thing the gauge is about with the lamp on and hear the hiss, read the lamps down the branch, scan with the chargeless scanner, find a second gauge elsewhere, ask a saner crewmate (dormant slice), or wait out the lie, which is the dearest rung because the real fault keeps bleeding the ship's air. No check shows a verdict, a card or a "you were hallucinating" line; the world settles it by being itself. Camera, controls, lamp, aim, movement, prompts, durations and the save and pause surfaces do not change at any stage.

**What the game loses without it.** Pillar 4 is the hook ("misinformation with a verification economy around it", not a stat debuff), and this system is the pillar's only mechanism. Without it, low sanity would be a movement or aim penalty, the exact outcome the pillar's secondary design test rejects ("-10% move speed" against "a gauge showing a false value": the false gauge); the Graph's falsifiability table (LSG LS-22), Suit's chargeless scanner, Interaction's `field_check` and the whole consequence channel would have nothing to be a witness *against*; and the friendly-harm design (death is lethal, including friendly fire, by user decision) would have no gate, no victim tell and no attribution, which is the difference between "a crewmate killed me because the web made them see a monster" and unconstrained PvP, an anti-pillar. The core hypothesis's fifth requirement (a single hallucination class, false system state, falsifiable by the physical world) cannot be tested without it.

**MVP scope.** `CLASSES_ENABLED` is a data bitmask; the MVP enables **class (b) only**. The rules for classes (a), (c) and (d) are written to the same depth and are testable against stubs, but every class-(a), (c) or (d) acceptance criterion is tagged deferred with a named blocker (Horror Entities, Perception Filter, Boarding & Salvage, Co-op). Suit readings stay honest in the MVP (SE-29), the scanner is an honest anchor, and a single seat has no crewmate, so the MVP ladder is rungs 1, 2 and 4 (the room, a second instrument, time). The harm gate bit is computed and queryable in the MVP but has no target.

**Index wording reconciled.** The index rationale (IDX Systems Enumeration bullet 19) is honoured with the corrections below, each recorded as a refresh owed and never as an edit.

| The index says | This GDD does | Why | Refresh owed |
|---|---|---|---|

**Reading guide.** Detailed Design holds the named rules `SN-1` to `SN-30` (Core Rules), six state tables (stage, victim-tell flag, harm gate, lie lifecycle, compartment-entry `entry_pending`, seat lifecycle) and one Interactions table per neighbour with the dependency GDD's own row quoted. Numbers are `[PLACEHOLDER]` unless a registry name says otherwise; every locked constant (`TICK_RATE`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, `HP_OK`, `HP_FAIL`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S`, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `HARM_TOTAL_BP`, `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER`, `DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX`, `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, `STEP_BUDGET_MS`) is cited by name with its registry value and never restated with a different one. Where this GDD adds a rule the orchestrator's rulings left open it carries an `(orchestrator ruling: ...)` note; where it adopts a specialist proposal that amends a ruling it is listed in Open Questions rather than silently applied. Neighbours with no GDD, and Horror Entities and Web Map & Transit (both now Designed), are marked `(provisional — <dep> undesigned/in flight)`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

## Player Fantasy

**Your eyes are an instrument you cannot recalibrate.** You are a technician keeping a dead ship alive, and the hardest instrument to keep true is your own sight. The power you earn is not strength. It is knowing when to doubt what you see, and being able to afford to check.

The concept states the horror precisely: "The horror is not that the web attacks the ship. It is that the web attacks the crew's ability to know whether the ship is fine." Sanity here is therefore not a second resource beside the ship. It is the ship's legibility turned against its keeper. Most survival games hand the player a bar to protect, a thing the game does *to* them. Here the player owns no number and suffers no penalty. What they own is a **verification economy** (the concept's phrase: "misinformation with a verification economy around it"), and their competence is measured in how cheaply and correctly they can ask the world a question. The competence the game promises at its core is "learning when to doubt your eyes", and this section makes that clause the emotional centre of the system.

**Experience targets (concrete moments, not adjectives).**

1. *The glance that snags.* A gauge the player has read a hundred times has a needle that will not rest where a sane needle would. Nothing announces it. They have time to notice.
2. *The walk to the pipe.* They walk, lit, to the thing the gauge is about, and the world answers in its own language (a hiss, a draught, frost) without any interface confirming it.
3. *The cost they chose.* They decide whether this lie is worth two seconds and a tool swap, a walk, or a wait, and they can see the price before they pay it.
4. *Quiet certainty.* After a look at the room they know, and the gauge may still be lying: belief and the lie are different things. After a scanner check the lie itself ends and the needle glides home. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the rules' reading is adopted. A room-rung observation calls nothing and leaves the lie standing; a `field_check` DEBUNKS it, the needle returns to the truth on the glide path and the subject is immune for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`, SN-17.)*
5. *(Deferred, VS-B)* *The friend who walks like a technician.* See the second anchor below.

### Anchor moment (MVP, solo, class b): "The needle that won't hold still"

Built from fault 1 of the MVP fault set (an O2 pinhole at a clamp, an exposed DUCT LEAK; LSG LS-26). Every true side of this scene sits on the consequence channel (LSG LS-14) or on a Suit body cue; the lie sits only on the module-body gauge and glyph. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the scene is **staged one hop from the fault**. The pinhole is in compartment X; the lying duct gauge is on the wall of the adjacent compartment Y, joined to X by an open hatch (one hop, inside `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX`). Y's own `band_o2` is OK. That is what keeps the lie legal and live: L-G (SN-15) ends a comfort-direction lie the moment the seat's own compartment leaves OK, so the earlier staging, a seat standing in the leaking room with its breath shortening, could never coexist with a live comfort lie. The Suit's first cue (breath shortens, visor mists) is the confirmation the player meets on stepping into X, and it arrives within the same 12-tick Sanity run as the lie's end. This is the **first costly lie** (lie 2 or later): the first lie of a world is a harmless alarm-direction preface on a fault-free gauge (SN-16, The first lie), and it teaches the gleam, the glide and the scanner before this beat can land.)*

| Beat | What the player sees and hears | What is true | What the player does | What it costs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Lit entry | The chest lamp sweeps into Y. The duct gauge reads in the OK band, and a violet gleam sits on the glass edge. The needle is not at rest: it glides slowly and never settles (the GLIDE signature, ART HR-5). | The duct has a pinhole leak in X. The gauge is module-body, so it is corruptible (LSG LS-14). Y's air is fine, so the player's own breath is steady. | Nothing yet. The player may or may not look at the glass edge. | Zero. The lamp was already on; lit entry is not a cost. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-staged in Y.)* |
| 2. The snag | A faint sound signature accompanies the needle (Adaptive Audio's family, disjoint from the tell, SN-25). Separately, the dust in the beam skates toward the open hatch (the `AIRFLOW` draught) and a thin hiss comes through its frame: consequence-channel witnesses, never corruptible (LSG LS-14). | X's air is bleeding through the pinhole and its band is falling; Y's is not yet, which is exactly why the lie is legal (L-G). The draught runs from Y into X because X is the low-pressure side. | The player notices the disagreement between the reading and what the room itself is doing. This is the stage-1 teaching beat. | Attention to notice. No game resource. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the disagreement is gauge against the room's witnesses, not numeral against breath.)* |
| 3. Choose a rung | Honest options exist, each with a visible price (rung table below). | The truth is available on three rungs in the MVP: the room (X, through the hatch), a second instrument that is **not** the Suit (the scanner, or another fixed gauge on the loop: `suit.o2` reads Y, agrees with the lie and is not credited for a comfort-direction lie, SN-15 L-2I), and time. | The player picks one. | See table. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* |
| 4. The room rung | Walking to the hatch with the lamp on the player hears the HISS rise and feels the draught strengthen; mist may form at the pinhole beyond it. If they step into X, their breath steps shorter and the visor edge mists: Suit body cues, never corruptible (SE-25), arriving as X's `band_o2` leaves OK. | The pinhole is real and has been all along. Entering X, the seat's own compartment now has a band outside OK, so the comfort lie on Y's gauge is INVALIDATED at the next run (L-G) and the needle glides back to the truth while the breath shortens. | Walk to the hatch with the lamp on and listen; stepping into X is the player's own choice and is priced by the Suit's ladder (`SUIT_MIN_WARNING_S`), not by the lie. | About 3.2 s for 8 tiles (384 px at `V_WALK` 2 px/tick and `TICK_RATE` 60) plus one hatch crossing (`T_CROSS_PROV`, D.7), walk noise only (`NOISE_PER_TICK` walk 1), lamp on (light is an Attention input). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* |
| 5. Certainty without ceremony | No icon, no card, no "you were hallucinating" line. The hiss simply is. | A room-rung look calls nothing: the gauge may still read OK with its gleam, the lie has not ended, the player's belief has. A scanner `field_check` is the rung that ends the lie for this seat (DEBUNKED, the needle glides home, `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` immunity). | Repair the pinhole (a real verb) or leave. | Repair time and TOOL noise per the repair verb (Interaction's). A completed repair delivers its true reason as the ordinary outcome after the full channel; it does **not** call `record_falsification` (Interaction IA-30 / OQ 32 declines that call), but it ends the fault's witness, which INVALIDATES the comfort lie at the next run (SN-17, L-W). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* |

**What this beat proves.** The player was never told anything. The sane palette told them *where* to look (violet at the glass edge), the world told them *what* (hiss, draught through the hatch), and the body confirms it on arrival (breath and visor in X). If they ignored all of it and trusted the numeral, the pinhole keeps bleeding the loop. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the stakes are stated honestly. A stage-1 comfort lie cannot kill. L-G ends its ledger entry the moment the loop's guard fails (the seat's own compartment band leaving OK for the O2 loop, SN-15's per-loop table for the others), and L-S keeps the lethal-mode faults (2 and 8) off stage 1, so the Suit's first cue always arrives within one Sanity run (0.2 s) of the lie ending in the ledger; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)* the displayed needle then glides back to the truth over 32 to 38 s, visibly leaving the false value while the breath shortens, and the Suit's body cue, which is never corruptible, is the truth the whole time. What trusting the gauge costs is the leak itself: the ship's O2 stock keeps draining, the failure spreads toward its dependants one hop per propagation run, and the repair is late. Lethality in the MVP belongs to the Suit's ladder and the Graph's fairness gate, never to the lie. The earlier wording ("a death walked into", "the leak keeps bleeding the room" as the price of trusting a comfort lie) overstated what the rails allow.)* That is the Pillar 4 contract in one beat: fair, cheap to check, costly to ignore, and bounded.

**Rung price table for this anchor.** The order is the Graph's (LSG:44): the room itself; a second instrument elsewhere on the ship; a saner crewmate (VS-B); time. The full per-fault table is formula D.7.

| Rung | MVP-live? | Cost in time | Light | Noise | Tool swap | Trust | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. The room | Yes | the walk to the hatch, about 3.2 s per 8 tiles plus a crossing | lit (the lamp is already on) | walk noise | none | none | The witness is guaranteed by rule L-W (SN-15): a comfort-direction lie is only eligible while the hiss and draught are active *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| 2. A second instrument | Yes | another fixed gauge on the loop costs a walk (D.7); the scanner costs `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` (2 s) of lit channel. `suit.o2` is free to read but is credited **only for alarm-direction lies**: for a comfort-direction lie it reads the seat's own (OK) compartment and agrees with the lie *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING)* | lit for the channel | TOOL (Interaction IA-30: pushed per accrued tick) | scanner: equip slot 3, 0 sim ticks (Suit SE-11), refused `BUSY` while carrying | none | Rung 2 always exists for a lie that is created (rule L-2I, SN-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: noise class and swap duration are now declared, OQ-SN-6 and OQ-SN-13 half closed)* |
| 3. A saner crewmate | No (VS-B) | conversation | none | voice | none | the cost is **trust**: believing a friend's word over one's own eyes | The human channel is never faked |
| 4. Time | Yes | wait out the lie's life, at most `LIE_LIFE_S` max `[PLACEHOLDER]` | n/a | n/a | none | none | Expensive by design: the real leak keeps bleeding the ship's air while you wait. This is the rung that teaches rungs 1 and 2 are worth paying for *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

### Second anchor (DEFERRED, VS-B, class c): "The friend who walks like a technician"

Not the headline; the MVP ships without it. It is recorded so the MVP's rules deepen into it rather than being replaced by it. A crewmate stands at a console, seen by an actor whose `harm_eligible` bit is set. They are drawn as a horror: a branching silhouette, iridescence, a violet fringe. But the rig is driven by the crewmate's real velocity, so it is "a horror that walks like a technician" (ART 5.5). They carry their **true Tungsten beam in its true shape** and speak in their **true voice**; the art bible names these as three redundant tells (beam shape, fringe, voice), any one of which suffices. The actor's cutter is in hand. Nothing happens unless the actor deliberately starts a lit `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` channel with a cutter or welder; a hallucination alone harms nobody. If they do, the act is lethal (user decision: "This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens."; `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is 1 in the registry) and the death is attributed to the web. The victim has been shown a violet fringe for at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` before eligibility. The fantasy line here is **not** "you killed your friend". It is: *the beam was theirs, the voice was theirs, and you swung anyway because the shape was wrong.* The player had the truth channel and did not read it. That is a fair, devastating, retellable story, which is the playtest signal the index asks for ("friendly-fire deaths told as stories and crews keep playing together"). It is a story-generating failure, not a power, and the section does not romanticise it.

### Pillar text served (verbatim)

- P4 (the pillar): "Low sanity changes what the player perceives, not just their stats." The class (b) line: "False system states. Broken reads as fixed, fixed as broken; the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite. The physical consequence (P1) stays honest."
- P4 verification clause: "**No lie is unfalsifiable**: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust. At least one channel of truth always remains. Solo play is supported through the first two checks; co-op adds the human ones."
- P4 design tests: "a hallucination that can never be disproved vs. one that costs time, risk or trust to disprove → the costly one." Secondary: "−10% move speed vs. a gauge showing a false value → the false gauge."
- P1, the clause that makes the fantasy bearable: "A pipe may *look* fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops." and "Subtle is allowed; absent is not."
- Intended tension: "Pillar 1 (legible) vs. Pillar 4 (lying). Resolved inside the game world: the physical ship is ground truth; instruments and perception are what can be wrong. The tension is the game."

### Design test sentence (this system's own)

> **A lie the game confirms with a verdict (an icon, a card, a "you were hallucinating" line, a cut to the truth) vs. a lie the world settles by being itself (a hiss, a draught, a mist) → the world settles it.**

Any proposed debunk presentation is run through this. If the player learns the truth from something the interface *says* rather than something the world *does*, it fails. It is the sanity-side twin of P1's "hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe" and it excludes the reveal register the art bible deliberately does not define. A corollary for tuning: *"it wasn't really broken" vs. "I should have looked at the edge" → the second.* A lie that leaves a player feeling cheated has failed; one that leaves them feeling they skipped a free look has succeeded.

### How the fantasy deepens, not breaks, under hallucination

**The ladder is the competence loop.** Each lie is a question with a priced answer. Stage 1 teaches the first rung (look at the gauge's edge, then at the room). Stage 2, when "the light lies", teaches that the *tool* of looking can be false, so the player re-learns the one anchor that never shifts: the physical radius their light actually reveals when they walk into it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the cone-reach lie is a Lighting decoy kind and ships at VS-A. The MVP stage-2 beat is narrower: a rim fringe at the seat's own cone (lie-bound: drawn only while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE; user ruling OQ-SN-21) and lies that reach the state glyph and the lamp-housing glow sprite, the housing's emissive frame, with the real beam and `lux` untouched. After the user's OQ-SN-38 ruling the MVP reaches stage 2 on a calm ring-1 sortie of 473.8 s or more, a calm ring-2 sortie of 391.4 s or more, a ring-3 sortie of 333.4 s or more, or a shorter sortie that draws two to three breach epochs, so the teaching beat is reachable in normal play; see the MVP reachable envelope in SN-29 and the derivation in D.8)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Stage 3 teaches that cross-checking is itself a skill: the player who has paid small prices for twenty minutes is the player who survives the dense stage. Competence grows exactly as lies grow.

**What keeps it from breaking** (each is a rule; SN-15 and SN-17):

1. *Every lie has a witness.* A comfort-direction lie (FALSE_OK, GLIDE toward health, a frame swap to the healthier frame) needs an active consequence-channel witness; an alarm-direction lie (FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE toward alarm, a frame swap to the worse frame) needs an honest positive cue of health from a closed list. Absence alone does not qualify. The player is never asked to disprove a lie with nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: stated by direction, SN-15 L-D and L-W.)*
2. *A second instrument always exists.* For one seat, no loop may have every instrument lying at once.
3. *Every lie leaks.* Nothing is a seamless lie.
4. *Immunity after checking.* A checked subject is immune to a re-lie for that seat for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`, whether or not it was lying, so a check is never a trap and a clean check leaks nothing.
5. *No reveal register.* Debunking is quiet. The true reason arrives only as the ordinary outcome after the full channel, never instantly, never as a card, never as a cut-to-the-monster.
6. *Certainty is not cure.* A `field_check` ends the lie for that seat: the needle glides home on the same path as any other end and the subject cannot lie to that seat again for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`; a look at the room (a walk, a listen) calls nothing and leaves the lie standing while the player stops believing it. The sanity level is unchanged by either. The checked gauge carries **no mark** of having been checked *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the rules' reading is adopted over "a checked lie is not removed from the gauge"; the two rungs differ, SN-17)*: a mark would be a verdict icon in disguise, and the player's memory is the instrument. *(Orchestrator ruling: adopted from the creative-director's risk 3.)*

**Death inside the fantasy.** MVP death handling is the Save/Load death stub: every bound seat DEAD reloads the last autosave, with no beat, no lights-out, no death screen. The level rolls back with the world (SL H3): death is not a sanity cure, and a player already low wakes still low. Identical actions after a death draw the same per-subject dice sequence, though not necessarily at the same moment (the threat draws around a restored seat re-key per restore epoch, X8, so the level and stage drift apart), priced by a death, as the scrubber that fails at minute four is in the physical world (SN-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*. Knowledge survives in the player's head and later in the Codex: Pillar 5, "failure costs ship and body, never knowledge". The emotional shape is Zomboid's: death is a bitch, it happens, and the lesson survives.

**Under co-op (VS-B, designed, dormant).** Per-seat independence: two players at different stages see different gauges, and the disagreement itself becomes the talk ("is that scrubber really green on your screen?"). The fantasy gains Fellowship without changing its spine: the third rung is the first with a second human in it.

### INTENSE, and "Doubt, not horror"

The user requires hallucinations to be INTENSE: phantom monsters, false broken and fixed states, teammates as monsters, phantom loot. The art bible's emotional target for the lie is "*Doubt, not horror.* The player should feel 'that's not right' before 'that's a monster'. The overlay never adds a mood of its own." These are different axes, not a conflict. **Intense in count, kind and reach; never in tempo, volume or screen-wide treatment.** Intensity is how many lies run at once, how far a lie reaches (gauge, then glyph, then the whole machine sprite at stage 3, "the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite"), how close a phantom comes, and what a believed lie costs. Doubt is the per-lie loudness and tempo: each individual lie is quiet, plausible and leaks at an edge, and the overlay's "energy" is unchanged from the host state, or the player would read sanity off the screen's energy instead of off the world. Stage 3 is the intense register (loop hues bleed violet, flesh warmth enters surfaces) and stays inside the flash clamp (PIPE PR-13, at most 3 flashes in a rolling 1 s). Intensity is never bought by touching a witness, adding a consequence-channel item (a false-broken sprite adds no scorch, no arc POP, no mist, no trail), or removing a leak. If playtest says "not intense enough", the dials are `LIES_MAX`, `LIE_GAP_S`, `ONSET_PM` and the stage thresholds, never a louder lie. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: INTENSE as a count and reach promise is a stage-2 and stage-3 promise, and the MVP's reachable stage envelope was mostly stage 1 when this note was written (SN-29 table, since recomputed). The user ruled OQ-SN-38 option (b) on 2026-10-27: `STAGE_ENTER[2]` is 55000 and `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1]` 53000, so the MVP's main band is stage 1 for the first minutes of a long sortie and stage 2 (`LIES_MAX` 2, glow-sprite swaps, comfort lies on faults 2 and 8) from 473.8 s of a calm ring-1 sortie, earlier in ring 2 and ring 3 or with breach epochs; stage 3 still needs events. Only class (b) ships at the MVP, so INTENSE as count and reach for classes (a), (c) and (d) is under-delivered by design, and the user accepts that (X11, D77; the Filter re-scopes its PF-27 to PF-30 stubs). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* D.11 also shows that the live-lie count at stage 3 is bounded by the compartment-entry rate (one onset per entry batch), not by `LIES_MAX` 4.)* *(Orchestrator ruling: the user's INTENSE wins on count and reach; the art bible's unchanged energy is a hard limit on tempo.)*

### Tone and language

Write like the art bible: concrete, physical, sparse. Preferred nouns: lie, leak, witness, edge, rung, reading, the room, the glass, the fringe. Preferred verbs: check, listen, follow, look at the edge, settle, hold, drift. The lie's default flavour is **comfort** (the art bible's "the lie is kinder than the truth": warm, soft, domestic, where the truth is cold and hard; "warmth that was not paid for with a Tungsten source is a symptom"), so comfort-kinds are weighted above fright-kinds at stages 1 and 2 and fright-kinds rise at stage 3 (`KIND_W[kind][stage]`, SN-16). **Banned in GDD prose and in any player-facing string:** meter, bar, a gauge of sanity, debuff, buff, insanity effect, jump scare, reveal, "you were hallucinating", "the truth is revealed", cure (sanity is recovered by settling, not cured), panic as a stat, screen shake as a sanity treatment. The internal integer `mSU` may be named in rules and formulas and is described as never reaching a player surface. A player-facing noun for the state does not exist; characters do not say "I'm going insane". The game's silence on the word is itself a design statement.

### Reference techniques

| Reference | ADOPT | REJECT and why |
|---|---|---|
| *Beyond the Aquila Rift* | The lie is warm and comfortable, the truth cold and hard; the tell is in the almost-right; unpaid warmth on steel as the lie's palette | The explicit reveal shot; the arachnid creature (forbidden by the art bible's no-bilateral-limbs rule); skin as a subject; eroticism as lure |
| *Pandorum* | Waking into wrongness; the hands-free chest lamp as the player's only truth tool (user decision) | Revealing the delusion as a plot twist; authored story beats (anti-pillar) |
| *Project Zomboid* | "Death is a bitch. It happens." Death as raw story material; patient, systemic dread | Skill-grind and kill rewards (anti-pillar) |
| *Eternal Darkness* | The idea that the game itself can feel unreliable | Fourth-wall effects (fake save deletion, fake crash). **Forbidden:** save and pause surfaces are never a hallucination class (SL H6) and the HUD frame is an anchor |
| *Amnesia*, *Don't Starve*, most survival sanity systems | The fear of a state you cannot see | A visible sanity bar; sanity stat penalties; screen-wide blur or vignette; a wobble that tells the player the number (violates IA-39, the P4 secondary test, SE-27) |
| Scripted-scare hallucination games | none | Authored or scripted lies and jump scares: the anti-pillar "not an authored story", and a cheap-death risk |
| *Alien: Isolation*, *Subnautica* | Audio-first dread, tells before threats (Pillar 2) | Phantom tells and false calm: **permanently rejected** (AT-17; OQ-AT-11 closed at the gate); Sanity never fakes an Attention tell or a calm |
| *Barotrauma* | The crew as each other's check on reality | Chat-only trust (VS-B only; the MVP solo has rungs 1, 2 and 4) |
| Apollo console / *Satisfactory* (art bible) | State lives in the sprite: frame, reading, number | Icon-only state; a verdict glyph for hallucination checks (IA F-3: the scanner result is "a reading, never a verdict icon") |

### Anti-pillar and guard-rail statements this system carries

| # | Guard rail | Binds | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | **No self-harm lever.** No verb, item or action lowers sanity. Falsification grants certainty, not sanity; recovery has no light or power condition. | SN-5, SN-6, SN-8, SN-17; anti-pillar "not a combat game" | CON risk on deliberate self-lowering |
| G2 | **No reward path for harm.** Harm earns the actor nothing: no level change, no XP, no loot, no recovery. | SN-13; anti-pillar "no intentional PvP" | IA-31 "No reward path" |
| G3 | **Harm is system-gated and web-attributed.** Possible only while the *attacker's* `harm_eligible` bit is set (one SIM bit, the same bit the class-(c) render reads), preceded by a victim tell of at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, recorded in `incidents` attributed to the web. | SN-10, SN-11, SN-13, SN-20 | CON P4(c) |
| G4 | **Harm is lethal by user decision.** `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is 1 (registry). The down-only fallback is that one Suit knob, set by the creative-director on the IDX:280 playtest signal; Sanity adds no second switch. | SN-12 | user decision |
| G5 | **No authored lies, no scripts.** Onset is rule-driven from the player's own actions; no authored hallucination records exist in any template. | SN-16, SN-24; DA-37 | CON anti-pillar |
| G6 | **No win state via sanity.** No completion, mastery tier or achievement surface; scars are a later system and never violet or flesh. | anti-pillar "not a game you win" | CON; ART 5.3 |
| G7 | **Darkness is never a sanity cost** (creative-director HARD CONSTRAINT): no unlit seat, unlit room, beam-off or "alone in the dark" input; recovery has no lamp condition. | SN-6, SN-8 | EM:95, EM:1117, LO-31 |
| G8 | **No sanity surface anywhere.** No number, bar, ring, icon, colour, vignette or HUD glyph on any surface, own or crewmate's; no self-tell on the body. The only signs are the world leaking at edges and the one-bit prompt. | SN-14 | ART 5.3, ART 7.0, SE-25, IA-39 |
| G9 | **No stat penalty from sanity.** No movement, aim, channel, damage or stamina value reads sanity. | SN-27 | PCI:42, PC-12 |
| G10 | **The consequence channel, the human channel, trails, geometry, anchors and Attention outputs are never touched.** A lie may not remove, alter or add to them. | SN-15 (L-C), SN-27 | LSG LS-14 |
| G11 | **Class (b) only in the MVP.** Classes (a), (c), (d) are designed to the same depth and gated by `CLASSES_ENABLED`; Suit readings stay honest. | SN-28, SN-19 | CON MVP definition |

*(Orchestrator ruling, framing: three framings were weighed. A, "your eyes are an instrument you cannot recalibrate", is adopted as the spine because it is complete in the solo class-(b) MVP and deepens under co-op. B, "crewmates are each other's instruments", is the co-op deepening, dormant until VS-B. C, "the ship is a body turning against you", is demoted to a register note for the stage-3 look, because it is a palette idea rather than a verification economy.)*

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Format: **Rule / Why / Edge notes**. Ids `SN-n` (the prefix is unused in any GDD or the registry; `HR-` is the art bible's id for its horror-rules and is not this system's). Units: one **run** is one `sanity.drift` invocation, `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s (5 Hz), where `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` equals the kernel divisor 12 by relation (KRN:54, KRN:202) and is not a knob (the `SUIT_HP_FULL` precedent); 1 s = 5 runs; **the run clock is absolute: `run = T div 12`**, where `T` is the kernel tick the kernel persists and exposes in `kernel.clock`; the hook fires only when `T mod 12 = 0` (offset 0, divisor 12), so at a hook `run` is exact and monotonic across save, restore and an unbound stretch, and every stored run stamp (`start_run`, `active_run`, `end_run`, `debunked_run`, `until_run`, the debounce stamps, the `repaired_recent[]` stamps (reserved: none are written at the MVP, SN-17 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2)*), `batch_end_run`, `last_onset_run`) is an absolute `run` and never a per-seat count; `stage_dwell` and `harm_below_runs` are **executed-run counters** (an unbound seat freezes them but not the stamps, so a rebind sees every stamped window elapsed as if the seat had stayed bound) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*; `mSU` is the milli-sanity-unit, an integer that never reaches a player surface. Every Sanity-owned number is `[PLACEHOLDER]` and registered as such; every cited constant carries its registry value and owner. "Seat" means a PlayerId (1 in single-player).

#### Block, hook and level

**SN-1. Three blocks, not one.**
*Rule.* Sanity registers exactly three state blocks. Each carries `schema_version` 1, `supported_versions` [1], a plain-data snapshot (no `Vector2i`, no `StringName` keys, no derived caches), explicit dirty keys, restore-idempotence, `default_state`, `post_restore`, and a two-phase `restore` returning `(ok, reason, detail)` from a closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reason set; an out-of-range stored value is a hard failure and is never clamped (SL-15, items 1 to 12). `persist_scope` is stated explicitly on both SIM blocks, which closes SL OQ-21 and satisfies the boot lint `SL_BOOT_SCOPE_MISSING`.

| block_id | class | scope | persist / persist_scope | repl_hint | restore_after | contents |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sanity.state` | SIM | PER_PLAYER (PlayerId) | true / **WORLD** | `SHARED_SUMMARY`; the projection is `{stage, tell_on, harm_eligible}` only | `[]` | `level` (mSU), `stage`, `stage_dwell`, `tell_on`, `harm_eligible`, `harm_below_runs`, `pending`, `last_tell_epoch`, `forced` (dev diagnostic, SN-28; outside the projection; always false in a release export) |
| `sanity.ledger` | SIM | PER_PLAYER | true / **WORLD** | `HOST_ONLY` | `[sanity.state, graph.state, layout.state]` and, *(Horror Entities, Designed; block `horror.state`, HE-3; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `horror.state` | `lies[]` {**lie_id**, slot, subject_ref `{stable_id, surface}`, kind, params, **direction**, state, onset_stage, start_run, active_run, end_run, debunked_run, leak_modes, first_lie}, `immune[]` {subject_ref, until_run}, per-subject lie visit counters (stream `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"`, a map pruned at the first hook run after the subject's destruction, since Sanity has no destruction callback), RNG counters (`sanity.lure`, `sanity.phantom`, `sanity.hide`), **`evented[]`** (the subjects that have had their one lit-entry event in the current visit; replaces `lit_prev[]`), **`batch_end_run`**, `entry_pending`, `debounce[]`, `repaired_recent[]`, `horror_enc[]`, `lures[]`, `lies_seen` (counted on a lie's PENDING to ACTIVE step), the `incidents` ring, **`last_onset_run`** (absolute `run`, set on the accept run and read by the `LIE_GAP_S` gate; initial value on first bind `-1`, the one permitted negative, meaning "no onset yet" so the gap gate passes; any other stored value is `0` to `2^53`, and a value below `-1` is `COUNTER_RANGE`) and **`last_cid`** (the Graph compartment id the seat occupied at its last executed run, EXTERIOR id 0 for none; the visit detector compares it with the current `cid(P)`) and **`next_lie_id`** (the per-seat `lie_id` mint, starts 1, SN-23 entry schema) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `direction`, `evented[]` and `batch_end_run` added, `lit_prev[]` replaced, the `lies_seen` increment point moved, SN-15 L-D and SN-16)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: `last_onset_run` and `last_cid` were read by the rules (SN-16 gap gate and visit definition) but held nowhere, so a restore reset the gap and the visit state; `last_onset_run` cannot be derived from `lies[]` because ended entries are removed. Both are in the SHAPE key set, AC-SN-2, 76, 98, 159 and the new AC-SN-173)* |
| `sanity.perception` | PERCEPTION_OUT | PER_PLAYER | **false** (derived) | `OWNER_PRIVATE` | none (rebuilt in `post_restore`) | the per-seat projection of the ledger (SN-23) |

*Why.* CR-12 blinds Sanity's own hook to `PERCEPTION_OUT`, so active-lie state must be SIM; a block carries exactly one `repl_hint`, and the level's summary must reach every client (the victim must see the attacker's tell) while the ledger must never reach another client. Splitting by `repl_hint` turns "no reader but Sanity and DIAGNOSTIC" into a block-level lint instead of a field-level one. *(Orchestrator ruling R2, ADR candidate: ledger placement.)*
*Edge notes.* The ledger is a deliberate Pillar 4 oracle risk: a sim system that read it would learn which surface lies. The lint is "no symbol of `sanity.ledger` is read outside `sanity/` except by the Debug Overlay as DIAGNOSTIC" (an AC in Acceptance Criteria). `evented`, `batch_end_run`, `entry_pending` and `debounce` are state, not caches: if rebuilt in `post_restore` from the world at the snapshot tick, a snapshot taken between hook runs could rebuild a different set than the uninterrupted run held, and the kernel's `state_hash` (G4, CR-13) would diverge. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `lit_prev` became `evented`, SN-16.)* PlayerId keys are stable seat ids, never `peer_id` (KRN:52, 100). *Closed reason sets (QA-11).* `SANITY_BOOT_*` (registry: `SANITY_BOOT_REASONS`), one per assertion: `LEAD_INVARIANT` (D.4 (i) to (iv)), `AMBIENT_FLOOR_MISSING`, `AMBIENT_FLOOR_INVARIANT` (D.8 (a) to (c)), `HOME_REFUGE_INVARIANT` (D.8 (d)), `SUB_CAP_INVARIANT` (D.8 (e)), `HARM_HYSTERESIS` (D.9), `THRESHOLD_ORDER` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: the threshold-ordering assertion `0 < SAN_HARM_ENTER < SAN_HARM_EXIT` and `SAN_TELL_ON + STAGE_HYST <= SAN_TELL_OFF` and `STAGE_ENTER[3] + 2 x STAGE_HYST <= STAGE_ENTER[2]` and `STAGE_ENTER[2] + 2 x STAGE_HYST <= STAGE_ENTER[1] < SAN_MAX`, so a retune can never invert a threshold pair)*, `IMMUNITY_INVARIANT` (`DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S >= max(LIE_GAP_S)`), `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS >= FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` and the floor a multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`)*, `REC_MAX_INVARIANT` (`REC_MAX = REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW`), `OVERLAY_BUDGET` (`PHANTOMS_MAX` plus decoy lies above `DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX`), `LIE_TARGET_DATA` (a `lie_targets.json` surface outside the module-body channel, a seam-lane valve or breaker entry (MSA F1), a lie kind with no leak, or *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)* a comfort-capable surface whose `loop` has no L-G guard row), `HIDE_STANDOFF` (`HIDE_SAFE_R` at or below Horror's `LURK_STANDOFF_PX`, SN-22). `SANITY_RESTORE_*` (registry: `SANITY_RESTORE_REASONS`), one per failure class: `SCHEMA_UNSUPPORTED`, `SHAPE` (missing or extra key), `LEVEL_RANGE`, `STAGE_RANGE`, `COUNTER_RANGE`, `LIE_KIND_UNKNOWN`, `LIE_TIME_ORDER` (not `start_run <= active_run <= end_run`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: `SUBJECT_UNRESOLVED` leaves the closed set. A well-formed `subject_ref` that no longer resolves after the Graph and Layout blocks restore is a legitimate dead ref, not corruption: a subject may be destroyed or salvaged between hook runs and the snapshot may be taken before the next run. It is handled by the next hook run (SN-23, EC-SN-30); only a malformed ref is `SHAPE`.)* A boot reason refuses boot; a restore reason fails that block's two-phase restore and leaves the pre-restore state unchanged. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync: the `SANITY_BOOT_*` reasons are raised by the start-up content loader before the kernel exists, so they are fault domain 2 (content data) codes in `ContentFaults` under the same names, and the composition root acts on them (ADR-0007 § 2.3); the `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reasons stay in Sanity's own domain 14.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: **JSON number typing.** A JSON-parsed integer arrives in GDScript as a `float`, so the SHAPE check accepts any integer-valued, finite float for an integer field and re-casts it to `int` before range checks and hashing; a fractional value, NaN or infinity fails `SHAPE`. This keeps `state_hash` parity (the hash is over the re-cast ints and sorted keys) without making a strict type check refuse every restore. AC-SN-76 carries the JSON round-trip and the two-insertion-order cases.)*

**SN-2. One hook and a fixed run order.**
*Rule.* One hook: `(hook_id sanity.drift, owner sanity, divisor 12, offset 0, after: [{graph.propagate.failure, tolerates_stale: true}, attention.step, emitter.place, body.integrate, interaction.step, {suit.vitals, tolerates_stale: true}, horror.step])`. All seven `after` ids exist on disk (LSG, ATT:129, Emitter State, BDY:140, Interaction IA hook table, Suit SE hook, Horror HE-4); an unknown id or a cycle fails boot (CR-3). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: under the Graph's baseline stagger (LS-8: `graph.propagate.power` (6, 0), `.atmo` (6, 2), `.failure` (6, 4)) and Suit's re-registration at (6, 2), `graph.propagate.failure` and `suit.vitals` never co-run with Sanity (12, 0), so both edges are declared `tolerates_stale: true` (kernel CR-3) and boot emits no never-co-running warning for `sanity.drift`; `attention.step` (6, 0), `emitter.place`, `body.integrate`, `interaction.step` (1, 0) co-run on every Sanity tick, and `horror.step` (5, 0) co-runs at T mod 60 = 0, so none of them needs a flag. `suit.vitals` (6, 2) never co-runs with Sanity (12, 0); Sanity reads the Suit's last committed values, those of the Suit run 4 ticks before every Sanity run (12m - 4), so a band change committed at a Suit run on tick 6k + 2 reaches Sanity 4 or 10 ticks later; the `.failure` values Sanity reads are those of the run 2 ticks earlier.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: `suit.vitals` (then registered at divisor 6, offset 0, co-due with Sanity at every `T mod 12 = 0`; superseded 2026-10-28, above) and `horror.step` (divisor 5, co-due when `T mod 5 = 0`) were co-due with no `after` edge, so registration order would have decided what Sanity reads at the shared tick. Sanity reads Suit's bands and cue-relevant state and Horror's `horror.view`; neither reads `sanity.state` in its hook (Suit never reads sanity, SN-19; Horror reads only the SIM view `sanity_view`, a committed projection of the previous run), so no cycle forms. Registry request on `sanity.drift`.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `interaction.step` is added. Interaction now calls `record_falsification` from `interaction.step` on the completion tick (IA-30), and that hook is `after: [controller.step, emitter.place]`, so without an edge the order of the two hooks on a shared tick would be a registration tie that CR-3 forbids relying on, and EC-SN-12, EC-SN-13 and AC-SN-66 and AC-SN-128 assume the falsification lands first. No cycle: nothing Interaction orders after reads Sanity's hook. Registry request on `sanity.drift`.)* Sanity registers no command, no `INPUT_SURFACE`, no PLAYER-origin anything in the MVP; its only writes are inside its own hook and its two Class B mutators. For each bound seat in ascending PlayerId, a run does, in this order: (1) snapshot inputs (Attention view, Graph reads, the seat's section and position, the lit set, `body_is_home`, `ring`); (2) D.1 drift: exposure, recovery, pending drain, level update; (3) D.2 stage, then the `tell_on` flag and D.3 harm gate; (4) ledger upkeep: PENDING to ACTIVE activations with witness re-check, EXPIRED, INVALIDATED, pruning of `immune[]`, and the INVALIDATE-and-prune of every ledger entry whose `subject_ref` no longer resolves (a subject destroyed or salvaged since the last run, or a dead ref carried through a restore, SN-23); (5) lit-entry events then D.5 onset (class b, and a, c, d if enabled); (6) rebuild `sanity.perception` in full from steps 3 to 5; (7) store the visit bookkeeping (`evented[]`, `batch_end_run`, `last_cid`) for the next run. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: `last_cid` joins the stored bookkeeping; D.5 writes `last_onset_run` at step 5.)*
*Why.* Reads must see this tick's Attention phase, lit set and seat section at the coincidence `T mod 12 = 0`, and the Graph bands and Suit state as last committed (the stale-tolerant edges above); `graph.propagate.power` and Attention (divisor 6, offset 0) land on that tick, the Graph's `.atmo` and `.failure` and the Suit (offsets 2 and 4) do not *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so Sanity's work is O(seats x small constant). `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is a logging threshold shared with them, not a cap. Offset 0 versus 6 is the kernel owner's to rebalance if the coincident hooks breach it.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C6: the kernel's fixture label `sanity (12, 0)` (KRN:787) is an example, not this registration; the real id is `sanity.drift` and a wording check is routed to the kernel owner (reflexion P-10: examples are read as contracts). Hooks may submit SYSTEM commands; Sanity submits none. `horror.step` runs at divisor 5 (HE-4) and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: is now in `after:`, so on the coinciding ticks it runs first)*; at a Sanity run `horror.view` is up to 4 ticks old since its last run, and up to **5** ticks counting the one-tick anchor lag of E8 (the bound earlier written as 4 undercounted the anchor lag; at most 5 x `V_HUNT_MPX`/1000 px of motion), which E3's 288 px radius and the HIDE collapse tolerate; Sanity never needs same-tick Horror state *(Horror Entities, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**SN-3. Level, units and seat lifecycle.**
*Rule.* `level` is an integer in `[0, SAN_MAX]` mSU, `SAN_MAX` 100000 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, set to `SAN_MAX` for a seat on its first bind in NEW_GAME. A seat first bound mid-game (VS-B join) starts at `SAN_MAX`; a PlayerId that already has stored state keeps it (no join-to-reset lever). Seat lifecycle (table in States and Transitions): an **unbound** seat does not drift, has no onset and retains its state under its PlayerId; a **DOWNED** seat drifts (exposure is real while down) and keeps onset; a **DEAD** seat freezes at the tick of death, and in the MVP death is the death stub (SL-7: reload the last autosave), so the frozen state is replaced by the restored one.
*Why.* Integer and JSON-safe (SL S-1), far below 2^53; the resolution (1 mSU per run) is below the slowest rate; the number never reaches a player (IA-39: "no sanity meter or number exists"). Death is not a cure (SL H3).
*Edge notes.* A guest joining a host world and whether a guest keeps a stage are Co-op's and Sanity Scars' (OQ-SN-4). *Integer conventions (QA-10; every boundary test needs the side of the equality).* On first bind `stage_dwell` is `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` (saturated), `harm_below_runs` and `pending` are 0, and `last_tell_epoch` is set to the live `tell_epoch`, so a stale BREACHED outcome at bind grants no startle; `last_onset_run` is -1 ("no onset yet", the gap gate passes) and `last_cid` is 0 (EXTERIOR, "none"), so the seat's first run in a real compartment opens its first visit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*. D.2 reads `stage_dwell` as stored by the previous run and the counter is updated after it (+1, saturating, or 0 on a change), so the first legal change is the run after the counter reaches 25. All time windows (immunity `until_run`, `REPAIR_BIAS_S`, `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S`) are exclusive of their end run (immune while `run < until_run`; biased while `run - stamp < 300`); `LIE_GAP_S` is inclusive (a gap of 225 runs passes at exactly 225); distance tests (`HORROR_NEAR_R`, `HIDE_SAFE_R`, `CREW_R`) are inclusive (`d <= R` counts).

**SN-4. Stages 0 to 3 with hysteresis and dwell.**
*Rule.* Stage is a pure function `stage_of(level, prev_stage, stage_dwell)` (D.2). Stage 0 sane; 1 the instruments; 2 the light; 3 the world's hue (ART 4.4) (registry: `sanity_stage`). Entry thresholds, strictly below: 1 at 70000, 2 at 55000, 3 at 25000 `[PLACEHOLDER]` (stage 2 raised from 45000 by the user ruling OQ-SN-38, derived in D.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; exit thresholds, strictly above: entry plus `STAGE_HYST` 2000 (72000, 57000, 27000). A change is blocked until `stage_dwell` has reached `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 (5 s). Harm eligibility and the victim tell are orthogonal flags (SN-11), never stages. **There is no stage 4.**
*Why.* The art bible has exactly three lie stages; PIPE:904 makes stage 3 the Filter's maximum; PIPE and PCI fixtures use 0 to 3. The SIM steps instantly; visual onset smoothing is the Filter's, subject to PR-13.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C1 routed to Failure & Trail: FT AC-88 "stages 0-4" must read "0-3". Two thresholds crossed in one run is impossible under the cap (36 mSU per run against gaps of at least 15000 between entry thresholds, after the OQ-SN-38 ruling; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: was 20000*) and arises only through a restore or debug force, where the stored stage is kept and a forced recompute moves directly to the target.

#### Inputs to the level

**SN-5. Exposure: a closed list of four true inputs.**
*Rule.* Exposure per run is `E = E1 + E2 + E3 + E4`. All are SIM values, true, read one way.
- **E1, depth and foreign body.** `RING_EXPOSURE[ring(p)]` (three depth rings 1 to 3, ring 1 the shallowest, Attention AT-6; `ring(p)` is `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, and an unknown ring resolves to ring 1) plus `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` while the seat's body is not the home body (home-ness is `body_is_home`, orthogonal to ring, because the home ship moves). E1 is subject to the ambient floor (SN-29). Web Map & Transit publishes `ring(PlayerId)`, `body_is_home(PlayerId)` and `locus_kind(PlayerId)` as SIM reads and states the baseline, ring 1 for HOME, derelict A, derelict B and the pod (WM-27) *(Web Map & Transit, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **In the pod** (the seat's `loco_state` is IN_POD, or its body is the pod): `aboard` is false, so `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is not charged; the ring is Attention's `held_ring` (WM-27), and `settled` is false. The hop is neither a refuge nor a drain beyond its ring term. This answers OQ-WM-6 (orchestrator ruling, logged). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **E2, Attention.** SN-7.
- **E3, real horror contact.** The seat's foot origin is within `HORROR_NEAR_R` 288 px `[PLACEHOLDER]` of the `pos_px` of a registered HORROR subject on the same body, with **no light condition**; the rate is `EXP_HORROR` per run while true, paid against a per-encounter budget (SN-30). Sanity computes this itself from `horror.view` positions; Horror publishes the facts and computes no exposure (HE-32, HE-40, AC-HE-101). Phantoms are never registered (SN-22) and so never feed E3. *(Horror Entities, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **E4, witnessed crew death.** VS-B, dormant: value 0 until Co-op exists.
*Why.* Legible, true, and every input already designed or provisional. The MVP target-derelict sortie drifts sanity even at Attention tier 0, through the aboard term, which is what lets the class-(b) hypothesis be tested in a 30-minute session.
*Edge notes.* E3 is **distance only** (checkpoint CQ7, closes OQ-SN-3): SN-6 forbids any light condition in an exposure term, and a lit-required E3 would turn darkness into a sanity reward and double-count Attention's restraint, so dousing the lamp near a horror neither raises nor stops E3. E2 is ship-global (every bound seat) in the MVP, which is moot with one seat; scoping E2 to seats near the Attention locus is a VS-B deferred item owned by Attention and Co-op because it needs a per-seat locus-distance or locus-body read Attention does not publish (preferred direction: seats on the locus body only; OQ-SN-2).

**SN-6. Forbidden inputs (HARD CONSTRAINT).**
*Rule.* The following never appear in any exposure or recovery term, in any mutator, or in any condition that changes `level`: **darkness in any form** (an unlit seat, an unlit room, the lamp off, the beam off, "alone in the dark"; EM:95 principle 8, EM:1117 OQ-4, LO-31); **Suit vitals** (hypoxia, cold, reserve; SE-27 bans a hypoxia hallucination); **the player's own noise** (it is Attention's input, so counting it here would double count); **any `PERCEPTION_OUT` value**; **any Perception, Lighting or Audio value**; and **player action counts or any verb or item that lowers sanity** (no self-harm lever). A source-scan lint of the D.1 level path (every symbol that can reach `level`, `pending`, or an exposure or recovery term) for these symbols, and one AC for each, are written in Acceptance Criteria. The lint scope is the level path only: the `lit` condition of SN-16 is an onset condition, not a level term.
*Why.* Pillar 3: going dark must stay a pure choice with no hidden price. Pillar 1: exposure is the web, not a stat the player manages by a side channel. The creative-director's constraint on this GDD is "that GDD must not make darkness (an unlit seat, an unlit room) a source of sanity loss, and must adopt or counter this text when it lands": it is adopted here.
*Edge notes.* The recovery term reads Graph compartment bands, not Suit vitals, and carries no light, power or lamp condition (SN-8). Any "you are alone in the dark" cue elsewhere is routed to Sanity as a non-draining cue and never a drain.

**SN-7. Attention coupling: yes, one-way, a rate by phase and tier.**
*Rule.* Sanity reads `attention.view` fields `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch`, and does **not** read `level_pm` (it is presentation-quantised, AT-26, and reading it would leak the hidden level). E2 per run: CALM 0; TELL `EXP_TELL[tier]` with tier 1 to 3 = 6 / 12 / 20 (tier 0 = 0); BREACH `EXP_BREACH` 28; SUBSIDING `EXP_SUBSIDING` 14. On a run where `phase` is CALM and `tell_epoch` differs from the stored `last_tell_epoch`, **overwrite `last_tell_epoch` with `tell_epoch` (always, whatever the outcome)** and, only if `tell_outcome` is BREACHED, add `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500 mSU to `pending` (capped, SN-9); during TELL, BREACH and SUBSIDING neither the epoch nor the outcome is read for this purpose, so a restore never double-credits a startle and a BREACHED outcome that lingers into the next TELL can never be credited twice. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED, closes OQ-SN-42: Attention publishes `tell_outcome` BREACHED only at the SUBSIDING to CALM transition (attention-system.md AT-16 row and CALM row), so the credit lands on the first CALM run after the breach, not at breach entry; the constant keeps its name `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` and its 1500 value (R10) and now names the startle of the breach, credited when the breach has ended. Sanity asks Attention to pin that `tell_outcome` resets to NONE at TELL entry (cross-GDD note); Sanity does not depend on it, since it reads the outcome only in CALM.)* Sanity never writes Attention and feeds nothing into its thresholds (ATT:374). The tell is a truth channel: **no phantom tell and no false calm at any tier or stage** (AT-17, permanent, closed at the gate by pillar).
*Why.* This answers ATT:374: "it states whether Attention events change sanity and by how much (Sanity's rule)". The loop Attention to sanity to lies to player noise to Attention passes through the human, never through the simulation, and is documented as intended.
*Edge notes.* The reader notes of Attention's `tier` and `tell_outcome` list Sanity (ATT:227-228) and ATT:380 reads `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch` with no `level_pm`; C11 is closed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: status refresh.)* The startle timing is settled by Attention's publication point (OQ-SN-42, closed 2026-10-27, re-review).

**SN-8. Recovery: a settled home, an idle trickle, a saner crewmate.**
*Rule.* `recovery R = (settled ? REC_SETTLED : REC_IDLE) + (crew_saner ? REC_CREW : 0)` with `REC_SETTLED` 12, `REC_IDLE` 1, `REC_CREW` 3 mSU per run `[PLACEHOLDER]`. `settled` is true when the seat's body is the home body AND every atmosphere band (`band_o2`, `band_p`, `band_t` of `compartment(cid)`) of the seat's compartment is OK AND Attention `phase` is CALM AND `tier` is 0. `crew_saner` (VS-B, dormant) is true when another bound seat within `CREW_R` is at least one stage saner. No light, power or lamp condition appears anywhere in recovery. A falsification grants no recovery (SN-17). `REC_MAX` = `REC_SETTLED` + `REC_CREW` = 15.
*Why.* It gives the home and sortie rhythm (Pillar 5) and never rewards or punishes darkness. Compartment bands are a Graph read, not Suit vitals.
*Edge notes.* **Home-refuge invariant (build assertion):** `max(RING_EXPOSURE) < REC_SETTLED` (8 < 12), so a settled home compartment nets a gain in any ring. A home compartment with a band outside OK loses `settled` and recovers only `REC_IDLE`; this is Graph truth. A home-base recovery floor (`REC_HOME_BASE`) is **not adopted** (checkpoint CQ3, closes OQ-SN-9; reopened only on a playtest signal): a home body has `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 0 and a home in ring 1 has E1 0, so net is at least 0 absent E2 and E3, deeper rings are bounded by the ambient floor (SN-29), and the cure for a damaged home is repairing one compartment to all-OK bands, which is Pillar 5's ship cost and a Pillar 1 loop.

**SN-9. One lowering path, the net cap and the pending pool.**
*Rule.* Only D.1 lowers `level` (the debug `force_level` and `force_stage` are the only exceptions; they live in the dev-only source root (`overlay_source_root`, `src/debug/`), excluded from the Release export by file and registered only through the Debug Overlay's gate (`overlay_build_gate`: file-level exclusion plus gated registration; the gate's feature-tag name is the Overlay's own placeholder and [UNV], and Sanity defines no tag of its own) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, SN-28). Net loss per run is `clamp(E - R + draw, -REC_MAX, +DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN)` with `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36 mSU per run (180 per second) `[PLACEHOLDER]`; the lower clamp is unreachable by construction (recovery is at most 15 and exposure is at least 0) and is kept as a build assertion a retune must not break. Startle and every future one-shot enter `pending` and drain only into the headroom left under the cap; the drain floor is 0. `PENDING_MAX` 3000 mSU `[PLACEHOLDER]`; an addition beyond it is discarded (overflow only ever reduces drift, the safe direction). *(Orchestrator ruling: SD proposed 4500, GD 3000; 3000 is adopted, two stacked startles.)*
*Why.* If startle bypassed the cap, a BREACH plus a horror in the beam could drop 55000 mSU in under a minute and unlock lethal friendly harm without minutes of lies, breaking the concept's "crossing the threshold must be slow and costly". Routing every pulse through the cap makes the lead invariants of SN-10 true build assertions rather than hopes.
*Edge notes.* The rulings' "about 42 runs" to drain a startle (1500 / 36) holds only with zero other exposure. With exposure present the headroom is smaller: ring 1 aboard in BREACH (`E` = 20 + 28 = 48, `R` 1) is capped at 36 with headroom 0, so the startle waits until BREACH ends; SUBSIDING at home in ring 1 (`E` 14, `R` 1, because the phase is not CALM) leaves headroom 23. The cap and the invariants are unaffected; only that prose number changed. A debt that outlives the danger ("the hangover") is the intended feel, bounded by `PENDING_MAX`.

#### Lead invariants, the harm gate and the sinks

**SN-10. Lead invariants: the sanity twin of the fairness gate.**
*Rule.* Five build assertions (D.4 (i) to (v); (iv) adds the 150-run dwell to bound (ii); (v) is measured from the lowest ambient floor, *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), evaluated from the knob table at boot and re-run on every retune (formula `drift_lead_invariant`, D.4): (i) from stage-1 entry (70000) to `SAN_TELL_ON` (30000), at least `SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S` 220 s at the worst-case cap: 40000 / 36 = 1112 runs = 222.4 s; (ii) from `SAN_TELL_ON` to `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (15000), at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` 60 s: 15000 / 36 = 417 runs = 83.4 s; (iii) from stage-1 entry to `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, at least `SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S` 300 s: 55000 / 36 = 1528 runs = 305.6 s, and the bit sets on the 150th consecutive run below `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (SN-11), run 1677 = 335.4 s; **(v) from the lowest ambient floor** (`min(AMBIENT_FLOOR)` 43000, the ring-3 floor, where routine play actually starts) to `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, at least `SAN_FLOOR_TO_HARM_MIN_S` 180 s `[PLACEHOLDER]` value, the target confirmed by the user ruling on OQ-SN-43 (2026-10-27: three minutes from routine ring-3 play to lethal friendly harm) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* counting the dwell: 28000 / 36 = 778 runs = 155.6 s, plus the 150-run (30 s) dwell = 185.6 s. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: (i) to (iv) are anchored at 70000, but a seat that has been aboard starts its descent from the ring floors 53000, 52000 and 43000, so the real lead to the friendly-harm gate from routine play is 241.2 s from the ring-1 floor, 235.6 s from ring 2 and 185.6 s from ring 3, not 300 s. The ring-1 and ring-2 figures are not asserted separately: the ring-3 floor is the binding case. The ring-1 figure was 291.2 s before the OQ-SN-38 ruling lowered that floor from 62000; bounds (i) to (v) are otherwise unchanged by it, D.4.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* Corollary assertions: `harm_eligible` implies `stage == 3` and implies `tell_on` (outside debug force); `max(RING_EXPOSURE) < REC_SETTLED`; the ambient-floor invariants (SN-29). The GDD must show the **cap is the binding bound**: the worst-case uncapped exposure at ring 3 aboard in BREACH with a horror near is `8 + 20 + 28 + 20 = 76` (`RING_EXPOSURE[3]` 8, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20, `EXP_BREACH` 28, `EXP_HORROR` 20) against `R` = 1, far above 36, so `net` is 36 and the startle waits in `pending`; even without the horror term `8 + 20 + 28 = 56` exceeds the cap, and ring 1 aboard in BREACH alone (`0 + 20 + 28 = 48`) exceeds it.
*Why.* It is the sanity analogue of `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (LSG:144). A friendly-harm gate that could open inside minutes would make the guard rails (system gate, victim tell, web attribution) decorative. The concept asks that "crossing the threshold must be slow and costly".
*Edge notes.* The margins are thin: 2.4 s on (i), 5.6 s on (iii) and 5.6 s on (v); 36 is the largest cap that passes (37 gives 1487 runs = 297.4 s and fails (iii)). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: guidance for retunes. Prefer deriving `SAN_TELL_ON` and `SAN_HARM_ENTER` from the lead targets, (i), (ii) and (v), over lowering the cap: a lower cap flattens every breach and starves the startle drain, while a threshold move changes only where the leads start. `SAN_HARM_ENTER` is capped at 16035 by (v) as well as by (iii), which agree.)* **Any retune of a threshold, the cap, the divisor, an exposure rate or an ambient floor re-runs all five**; reflexion P-7 (an invariant written against "the default" must be re-checked whenever a later decision adds reachable states) applies to every Tuning Knob.

**SN-11. The harm gate and the victim tell: one SIM bit.**
*Rule.* `harm_eligible` sets after `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` 150 (30 s) consecutive runs with `level < SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000 and clears **at once** on any run with `level > SAN_HARM_EXIT` 20000; a run with `level >= SAN_HARM_ENTER` resets the dwell counter. `tell_on` sets on `level < SAN_TELL_ON` 30000 and clears on `level > SAN_TELL_OFF` 35000, with no dwell (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`). Interaction's query is ratified verbatim: **`actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold(player_id) -> bool`** returns `sanity.state.harm_eligible` for that PlayerId. It is a pure function of SIM data, never of `PERCEPTION_OUT` (IA-31 gate step 4; IA:454). A SIM read view `sanity_view(player_id) -> {stage, tell_on, harm_eligible}` (summary class, no raw level) serves the Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells and the Debug Overlay. **The class-(c) render level IS that bit**: the Filter reads `harm_eligible` from SIM and no `PERCEPTION_OUT` copy of it exists. This records IA-31 precondition (1) (AC-IA-126 half a). Half (b), that every real HORROR subject carries a `repel` declaration with the same `verb_name_key`, tool, prompt conditions, duration class, bar length, noise class and cue set, is Horror's: Horror's draft does not yet record it *(Horror Entities, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*: HE-39 declares only the dormant suppressant interface `repel(origin, T)` (a different thing), and HE-38 states that HORROR takes no verb at MVP and reserves the VS-B `repel` declaration fields (IA:1277) by name in its Open Questions only. **VS-B `begin_harm` therefore stays BLOCKED** until Horror's GDD records the plate-parity declaration; Horror acknowledges it as owed at Vertical Slice B (not MVP), and the precondition blocks any `begin_harm` story. The bit is per actor; Interaction separately requires a target of a different PlayerId. **Second reader (VS-B), ruled 2026-10-27 (D53, user ruling):** Modular Ship Assembly's `CUT_STRANDS` (MSA-18, ASK F2) is gated on the cutter's `harm_eligible` by default, so Assembly's `validate` reads the cutter's bit through the same query `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* It is a pure SIM read, the same bit, never a PERCEPTION_OUT copy, and a stranding it permits is recorded through `record_harm_event` attributed to the web. No other simulation reader is permitted. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
*Why.* The IA:270 and IA:454 leak argument: any render level different from the gate bit lets a plate on a "horror" tell it from a real horror at zero cost. The victim tell precedes eligibility by at least `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` through SN-10 (Pillar 2 in spirit).
*Edge notes.* A mid-channel flip is Interaction's `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check (AC-IA-88). The hysteresis band (5000 mSU) makes it rare, not impossible. A harm channel is `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` 204 ticks = 17 runs, so the level moves by at most 17 x 36 = 612 mSU during one. A flip therefore needs the actor to start the channel within 17 x `REC_MAX` = 255 mSU below `SAN_HARM_EXIT` while recovering (an actor leaving eligibility at a settled home); it cannot arise from drift at the entry edge. *(Correction: the game-designer's claim that a natural flip is impossible holds only for the entry edge; QA should test the exit edge.)* In the MVP (single seat) the bit is computed and queryable but no target exists; class (c) rendering is disabled by `CLASSES_ENABLED` and exercised with stub actors. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

**SN-12. Lethality and the revert switch: one switch, one owner.**
*Rule.* Sanity records the user decision verbatim ("This is like project zomboid, death is a bitch. It happens."): a completed act (every step landed, `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000 >= `SUIT_HP_FULL` 10000) is lethal through Suit; when and how death arrives is Suit's (SE-23, SE-24; IA-31). `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` is **1** in the registry (Suit CK-2). The down-only fallback is that single Suit knob, changed by the creative-director on the IDX:280 playtest signal; Sanity adds no second switch, and this GDD never restates the old pre-decision value as a default.
*Why.* One switch, one owner (the registry's exception note). The index's high-risk entry reads: "Lethal friendly harm and verification cost must feel fair; no unfalsifiable lies. Playtest signal: friendly-fire deaths told as stories and crews keep playing together; revert to down-only if not."
*Edge notes.* Conflict C3 routed to Interaction: IA:270, the IA:891 knob row and IA:1345 OQ 3 still describe the pre-decision state of that knob; their refresh is owed.

**SN-13. The harm-event sink and web attribution.**
*Rule.* `record_harm_event(actor, victim, tick)` is a **Class B** mutator (clamp-never-reject) that Interaction calls from its `interaction.step` hook on the harm channel's completion tick (Interaction IA-31 and its call-site wording), under the hook's WriteScope (sim systems never subscribe, CR-10; the mutator is infallible, CR-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: "inside its apply WriteScope" was stale; `apply` runs at the begin tick. The registry text of `record_harm_event` still says "inside its apply" and is a registry request)*. It appends `{tick, actor, victim, attributed_to: WEB}` to the `incidents` ring (`INCIDENTS_MAX` 16 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, oldest dropped) and changes **nothing else**: no level change for the actor, no reward, no recovery, no E4 (a witness's E4 is VS-B). Scars, logs and consequences belong to Sanity Scars, the Codex and the Failure Contract, which read the ring.
*Why.* IA-31 "no reward path"; attribution to the web keeps friendly harm a story the web told and not an intentional PvP loop.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C10 routed to Interaction: IA-31 says it "emits one `IA_HARM_EVENT`"; the legal form is a call to this mutator, a wording refresh and not a rename. **Class B robustness (clamp-never-reject, CR-9; also `record_falsification`, SN-17):** a call with an unknown PlayerId or actor, an unresolvable `subject_ref` or an unknown `check_kind` is a no-op with exactly one log line, never a FAULTED mutation and never an exception (QA-12).

**SN-14. Self-knowledge: Sanity creates no new self-sign.**
*Rule.* The one-bit prompt (the creative-director's self-knowledge ruling, IA:270 and IA-39: a prompt or an immediate `NOT_ELIGIBLE` is "a one-bit statement about the actor's own sanity relative to the harm threshold. That is honest self-knowledge, which Pillar 4 allows") is the only hard self-knowledge. The diegetic sign is the stage leaks the actor already sees in the world: the stage-1 gleam on instruments, the stage-2 fringe at the cone rim (drawn while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE, lie-bound; user ruling on OQ-SN-21; **"stage-2 reach lie ACTIVE" is the predicate `reach_lie_active(P)` of SN-23: the seat's `stage >= 2` and a listed, activated `sanity.perception` entry with `away` false (its subject in the seat's current compartment) and `reach_tier == 2`** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING)*), the stage-3 hue bleed (default off; user ruling on OQ-SN-22) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, and, at eligibility, crewmates drawn as horrors. Nothing on the actor's body or HUD (ART 5.3: "there is no self-tell"; ART 7.0).
*Why.* It answers IA OQ 29 (a recommended, non-binding diegetic sign) as "the leak at the edge of the light is the sign", without a body sign or a meter.
*Edge notes.* Conflict C2 closes as "satisfied by existing leaks". The concept's P4(c) phrase "the attacker's palette leak or a visible sanity sign" describes the **victim-side** fringe (the attacker's leak seen by others, ART 5.5), not a self-sign.

#### Lies: eligibility, class (b), falsification, the ladder

**SN-15. Lie eligibility: the guard rails every lie of every class passes.**
*Rule.* A lie is created only if **all** of the following hold at activation; L-C, L-W, L-A and L-G are re-checked on every later run, L-S against the lie's stored `onset_stage`, and L-2I only for the loss of a fixed gauge it relied on (both below); a failing re-check INVALIDATES the lie (SN-16). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: L-W, L-S, L-G and the L-2I credits were written for FALSE_OK only, which left a GLIDE toward health, or a frame swap to the healthier frame, on a lethal-mode subject at stage 1 with no witness, no severity ceiling and no atmosphere guard. They now apply by the lie's **direction**, L-D, whatever its kind is called.)*

**L-D, direction (the table of record).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* Every lie has an *effective direction* computed at the accept from its kind and the subject's true state, stored on the ledger entry (`direction`) and re-derived every run: **COMFORT** (the display says the subject is healthier than it is), **ALARM** (the display says it is worse than it is). A lie whose display would equal the truth has no direction and is not a lie (INVALIDATED, EC-SN-38). Healthy means `hp >= HP_OK` 7000, DEGRADED means `HP_FAIL` 3000 < `hp` < `HP_OK`, FAILED means `hp <= HP_FAIL`. The rails that apply, by direction:

| Kind and surface | True state | Direction | L-C, L-L, L-I, L-A | L-W witness | L-S ceiling | L-G guard | L-2I credits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FALSE_OK | DEGRADED or FAILED | COMFORT | yes | an active consequence-channel witness of the fault | yes | yes | a fixed gauge on the loop; the scanner (carried and equippable, see L-2I) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| GLIDE (drift toward the comfort side) | DEGRADED or FAILED | COMFORT | yes | as FALSE_OK | yes | yes | as FALSE_OK *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| SPRITE_SWAP to the healthier frame (a rotor spinning on a stopped machine; a housing shown whole when cracked; the glow frame lit on an unpowered housing) | stopped, cracked or unpowered (DEGRADED or FAILED) | COMFORT | yes | as FALSE_OK | yes | yes | as FALSE_OK *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| FALSE_FAIL | healthy | ALARM | yes | an honest positive cue of health from the closed `health_cues` list | no | no | a fixed gauge; the scanner; `suit.o2` or `suit.warmth` for the seat's current compartment (valid quantities only) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| GLIDE (drift toward the alarm side) | healthy | ALARM | yes | as FALSE_FAIL | no | no | as FALSE_FAIL *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| SPRITE_SWAP to the worse frame (a housing shown cracked on a whole one; a machine shown stopped while running; the glow frame unlit on a powered housing) | healthy, whole, running | ALARM | yes | as FALSE_FAIL | no | no | as FALSE_FAIL *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
