Classes a, c and d are specified to the same depth, testable with stubs, and their ACs are tagged deferred with a named blocker (Horror, Filter, Boarding, Co-op). If class (b) is turned off at runtime, live lies expire normally and no new onsets occur, with no snapshot format change. Debug-only `force_stage(player, stage)` and `force_level(player, mSU)` live in the dev-only source root (`overlay_source_root`, `src/debug/`), excluded from the Release export by file and registered only through the Debug Overlay's gate (`overlay_build_gate`: file-level exclusion plus gated registration; the gate's feature-tag name is the Overlay's own placeholder and [UNV], and Sanity defines no tag of its own) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (registry: `overlay_build_gate`, `overlay_source_root`; a runtime debug flag is rejected because release ACs inspect the export file list), are reached from the dev console and displayed by the Debug Overlay (display only, DO-1, OQ-DO-11), are never a player surface, bypass the dwell and the cap, and are the only exception to SN-9; a forced recompute moves directly to the target stage with the dwell saturated, leaves the ledger untouched, and sets the seat's **`forced`** diagnostic flag (a per-seat field of `sanity.state`, outside the `SHARED_SUMMARY` projection, persisted so a restored forced save still reads FORCED, cleared only by NEW_GAME, and always false in a release export, where no `force_*` symbol exists); the Overlay's FORCED row shows it (OQ-DO-4). The Debug Overlay reads `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger` as DIAGNOSTIC (true value beside perceived) and shows telemetry counters (`eligible_entries_per_min`, the measured compartment-entry rate and surfaces lit per entry, lies shown, acted on, debunked, expired unseen, and *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* ended by L-G or by the witness ending, so the rung mix of AC-SN-165 can be read), so the reference rates of D.11 can be replaced by measured ones; none of this is ever an accessibility feature. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: **Godot export hazards.** (1) The runtime data files under `assets/data/sanity/` (`lie_targets.json`, `safe_ranges.json` where shipped, the threshold tables) are not imported resources, so an export drops them unless the preset's `include_filter` lists them (or they are authored as `.tres`); the boot assertions would then pass in the editor and fail only in the export, so the export preset must list them and AC-SN-178 boots the exported build. (2) "Excluded by file" debug code breaks the export if any shipped script `preload`s, type-references or `class_name`-references anything under `src/debug/`; the debug registration is therefore reached only through a runtime `load()` guarded by `OS.has_feature(<the Overlay's gate tag>)` with **no static reference** from shipped code, and AC-SN-179 is a CI check that exports a Release build and scans the PCK for any `force_stage`, `force_level` or `src/debug/` path.)*
*Why.* The MVP must be testable in a 30-minute session, so QA needs to put a tester at stage 2 at will.

#### Calibration rules

**SN-29. The ambient floor: calm waiting never opens the gate.**
*Rule.* Ambient exposure E1 (`RING_EXPOSURE[ring]` plus `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`) contributes only while `level > AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]`; below the floor E1 is 0 and recovery lifts the level back toward it. E2 and E3 are **not** floored. `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` = 0 / 4 / 8 and `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 mSU per run (safe range 8 to 20), `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]` = 53000 / 52000 / 43000 mSU (the ring-1 floor lowered from 62000 by the user ruling on OQ-SN-38; derivation in D.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `AMBIENT_MARGIN` 5000 (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`; rings are Attention's AT-6 indexing with 1 the shallowest, the ring is `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, a home body has `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 0, and a home in ring 1 has E1 = 0). Three build assertions (formula `ambient_floor_invariant`): (a) `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR) >= SAN_TELL_OFF + AMBIENT_MARGIN`, so ambient exposure alone can never set `tell_on`, enter stage 3 or reach `harm_eligible`; (b) every floor lies at least `STAGE_HYST` from every stage entry and exit threshold, so a floor parked on a boundary never chatters the stage; (c) the floor is non-increasing with ring, so deeper is never safer (Pillar 5). A ring with no floor refuses boot with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason; a body whose ring is unknown (an unresolved section, or a ring below 1 that Web Map's block rejects at `post_restore`) uses ring 1's floor (the AT-6 fallback) and logs *(Web Map & Transit, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
*Why.* *(Orchestrator ruling: additive to R6; the values stay placeholders.)* Without floors, at `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 and `REC_IDLE` 1, a player standing quietly aboard a derelict nets 19 mSU per run: stage 1 after 1579 runs (315.8 s), stage 3 after 75000 / 19 = 3948 runs (789.6 s, 13.2 min), and `SAN_HARM_ENTER` after 85000 / 19 = 4474 runs (894.8 s, 14.9 min) plus the dwell, with no danger at all. That contradicts the rulings' own sentence that stage 3 is reachable "only by sustained BREACH or horror contact" and the concept's "slow and costly". The floor makes the descent to stage 3, to `tell_on` and to `harm_eligible` require events (Attention, horror), never waiting. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: with the ring-1 floor at 53000 and the stage-2 entry at 55000, stage 2 itself can now be reached by waiting alone in every ring; that is the intended, ruled consequence of OQ-SN-38 option (b), and it is bounded by the same floors, which stay above `SAN_TELL_OFF` + `AMBIENT_MARGIN`.)*
*Edge notes.* The calibration hypotheses (checkpoint CQ5) are `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20 giving stage 1 after 1579 runs = 315.8 s (about 5.3 min aboard at tier 0) stage 2 (`STAGE_ENTER[2]` 55000) after `ceil(45001 / 19)` = 2369 runs = 473.8 s (7.9 min) and the ring-1 floor 53000 after `ceil(47000 / 19)` = 2474 runs = 494.8 s (8.2 min) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; a settled home recovers 30000 mSU in 2500 runs (500 s); both are **playtest hypotheses, not facts**, and the safe range of `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is 8 to 20 (playtest decides; this converts OQ-SN-1 into a knob range). A damaged home's worst ambient state is its ring's floor (CQ3), never lower without events. A seat returning home from a sortie that ended at the ring-1 floor (53000, stage 2) needs 334 runs (66.8 s) to cross the stage-2 exit at 57000 and `ceil(19000 / 12)` = 1584 runs (316.8 s) to cross the stage-1 exit at 72000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, so **lies on the home ship during that "hangover" are intended**: the player verifies in the place with every fault class's witnesses, and the consequence there is a home-ship repair, not a death. **Nesting with the sortie envelopes (Boarding C-BS-12):** the five-minute design target (300 s) <= stage-1 entry 315.8 s (15.8 s of slack) < Web Map's `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks (480 s) < `CELL_S`; a sortie cut at the fuel-floor bound spends about 164 s in stages 1 and 2 (158.0 s in stage 1, then the last 6.2 s in stage 2, which opens at 473.8 s). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: **the premise is corrected.** `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` is Web Map's fuel-floor guarantee, "the sortie the floor is meant to survive ... not a limit on visit length" (web-map-and-transit.md T_safe_steps and knob rows), and Web Map's D.10 visits run 14 to 18 minutes. The 480 s bound is therefore the shortest sortie the fuel floor guarantees, not the visit length, and "only the last 6.2 s in stage 2" described the wrong quantity. On a real visit spent entirely aboard at tier 0 with no events, the stage-minutes are: ring 1, 14 min = 315.8 s stage 0, 158.0 s stage 1, 366.2 s (6.1 min) stage 2, and 18 min = 606.2 s (10.1 min) stage 2; ring 2, 14 min = 261.0 / 130.4 / 448.6 s (7.5 min stage 2), 18 min = 688.6 s (11.5 min); ring 3, 14 min = 222.4 / 111.0 / 506.6 s (8.4 min), 18 min = 746.6 s (12.4 min). A calm seat therefore spends roughly 6 to 12 minutes of every visit in stage 2, so the Pillar 4 comfort lie is likely reachable in visit 1, and the first costly lie is not a "sortie 2 or later" event. The ruled pair 55000 / 53000 (OQ-SN-38) is **not reopened**; the real pacing risk is rest length against session length: a 14 to 18 minute visit followed by the 13.1 / 19.6 / 39.2 minute rest to `SAN_MAX` is a 27 to 31 minute cycle at a ring-1 home and 53 to 57 minutes at a ring-3 home, so a player who skips the rest begins the next visit already in stage 1 or 2, which is OQ-SN-44's playtest question.)* Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D73): the concept's five-minute W_LOOP is kept, Boarding budgets about 200 s of discretionary dwell, and entering sanity stage 1 on a long sortie is intended (the five-minute sortie itself stays in stage 0 without events) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. That is accepted: the fuel floor is Web Map's and looser than the sanity budget, the world shows the sanity cost and no timer does, and `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` is not retuned for it (OQ-SN-30 keeps it a playtest hypothesis).

**MVP reachable envelope** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the stage envelope the rules above actually deliver was never stated; it is arithmetic from D.1 and D.8 plus Attention's placeholder timings, `TELL_MAX_TICKS` 1800, `BREACH_MIN_TICKS` 900, `BREACH_MAX_TICKS` 3600, `SUBSIDE_TICKS` 1200, which are [UNV] here and Attention's.)* One breach epoch (a TELL of about 20 s at tier 2, a BREACH of 15 to 60 s, a SUBSIDING of 20 s, with the 1500 mSU startle credited at SUBSIDING end and draining over the next 32 to 89 runs, D.1 example E) costs roughly 6,000 to 12,000 mSU below the ambient floor under the cap (estimate from D.1: TELL 1,100 + BREACH 2,025 to 8,100 + SUBSIDING 1,300 + startle 1,500 = 5,925 to 12,000; the total is unchanged by moving the credit from breach entry to SUBSIDING end, only its timing moved; the cost is dominated by the BREACH length) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the table below is recomputed for `STAGE_ENTER[2]` 55000 and `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1]` 53000; the 6,000 to 12,000 mSU epoch estimate is unchanged and stays [UNV] until Attention republishes its timings after the X3 and X4 rulings.)*

| Scenario (start `SAN_MAX` unless stated) | Stage reached | `LIES_MAX` | Eligible kinds | Comfort lie on faults 2 and 8 (`LETHAL_FAULT_MIN_STAGE` 2)? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Calm ring-1 sortie from `SAN_MAX` (floor 53000): stage 0 until run 1579 (315.8 s), then stage 1 until run 2369 (473.8 s) | **1** | 1 | FALSE_OK (DEGRADED non-lethal subjects only), FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE; no SPRITE_SWAP | no *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Calm ring-1 sortie of 473.8 s or longer (stage 2 at run 2369, level 54,989; the 53000 floor first reached at run 2474 = 494.8 s, after the 480 s fuel-floor guarantee and well inside a 14 to 18 minute visit) | 2 | 2 | adds SPRITE_SWAP of a glow sprite (weight 5) | yes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Calm ring-2 sortie (floor 52000; stage 1 at run 1305 = 261.0 s, stage 2 at run 1957 = 391.4 s) | 2 | 2 | as above | yes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Calm ring-3 sortie (floor 43000; stage 1 at run 1112 = 222.4 s, stage 2 at run 1667 = 333.4 s) | 2 | 2 | as above | yes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Ring 1, a 300 s sortie (level 71,500, stage 0) plus 2 to 3 breach epochs (at least 16,501 mSU below 71,500 to cross 55,000) | 2 | 2 | as above | yes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Stage 3 (level below 25,000): ring 1 plus 3 to 5 breach epochs (28,000 mSU below the 53000 floor), ring 2 plus 3 to 5 (27,000 below 52000), ring 3 plus 2 to 3 (18,000 below 43000) | 3 | 4 | all kinds, machine-sprite swaps | yes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| Any scenario using `force_stage` | 1 to 3 | per stage | per stage | forced-state only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

Consequence, stated plainly *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: this paragraph records the user ruling on OQ-SN-38, option (b), 2026-10-27, and replaces the earlier "effectively stage 1" reading)*: with `STAGE_ENTER[2]` 55000 and `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1]` 53000, stage 2 is reachable in a normal sortie without a ring-3 descent or a breach epoch, but late: a calm ring-1 sortie crosses 55000 at run 2369 (473.8 s), 6.2 s inside Web Map's 480 s fuel-floor guarantee (not a visit length: D.10 visits run 14 to 18 minutes, so the seat spends 6 to 10 minutes of a calm ring-1 visit in stage 2, see the corrected premise above) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*, so a five-minute sortie is stage 0 without events and stage 1 only on a long one (D73, above). The MVP's stage-2 teaching beat (`LIES_MAX` 2, glow-sprite swaps, comfort lies on faults 2 and 8) therefore arrives on a long ring-1 sortie, on a ring-2 sortie of 391.4 s or more, on a ring-3 sortie of 333.4 s or more, or on a ring-1 sortie that draws two to three breach epochs. Stage 3 still needs events (3 to 5 epochs in ring 1 or 2, 2 to 3 in ring 3). Every D.7 row and AC that needs stage 2 is therefore reachable in play and no longer forced-state only; the stage-3 rows stay forced-state only unless the scenario draws the epochs above. The derivation of the pair (reachability of the 54,400 end-of-envelope level, the D.8 (b) clearance of 2000, and the full re-run of D.4 (i) to (v) and D.8 (a) to (e)) is in D.8. A side effect, stated and not hidden: because the ring-1 floor now lies below the stage-2 entry, **the first 66.8 s of rest at a ring-1 home after a sortie that ended at the floor are still stage 2**, so a comfort lie on faults 2 and 8 or a glow-sprite swap can occur on the home ship, where every fault class's witnesses are present (the hangover beat, edge notes above).

**Per-home-ring rest budget** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: pacing assumed every sortie starts at `SAN_MAX` and that rest is free, but the climb back depends on the home ring. Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: recomputed for the OQ-SN-38 pair; the budget the MVP assumes is the "as computed by D.4" set below, which replaces the earlier 10.6 / 15.8 / 31.7 minutes.)* Settled-home recovery above the ring's floor is `REC_SETTLED - RING_EXPOSURE[ring]` (12 / 8 / 4 per run for a home in ring 1 / 2 / 3; 12 at or below the floor, where E1 stops). The worst case starts from the ring-1 floor of 53000 (stage 2); a calm sortie cut at the 480 s fuel-floor bound ends at 54,400 (a full 14 to 18 minute visit ends at the floor) and every figure below is shorter by about 23 s to the stage-1 exit (`ceil(17600 / 12)` = 1467 runs = 293.4 s from 54,400 at a ring-1 home). Exits are strictly above their thresholds, so an exact division adds one run:

| Home ring | Net per run above its floor | To stage-2 exit 57000 | To stage-1 exit 72000 | To `SAN_MAX` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (the MVP baseline, WM-27) | 12 | 334 runs = 66.8 s | 1584 runs = 316.8 s | 3917 runs = 783.4 s (13.1 min) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 2 (floor 52000; the departing 53000 is above it, so E1 is live) | 8 | 501 runs = 100.2 s | 2376 runs = 475.2 s | 5875 runs = 1175.0 s (19.6 min) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 3 (floor 43000) | 4 | 1001 runs = 200.2 s | 4751 runs = 950.2 s | 11750 runs = 2350.0 s (39.2 min) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

A seat that leaves home early, at 72000 (just stage 0), reaches stage 1 after 2000 / 19 = 106 runs = 21 s of calm ring-1 sortie, so lies can start almost at once; a seat that rests to `SAN_MAX` gets 315.8 s of grace before stage 1 and 473.8 s before stage 2 in ring 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The rest the player takes is a pacing lever the player holds, and a home deeper than ring 1 multiplies it (Web Map's decision, WM-27). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**SN-30. Horror-contact budget: one horror cannot be farmed.**
*Rule.* E3 pays `EXP_HORROR` per run only against a per-horror **encounter budget** `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 mSU `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tracked in `horror_enc[]` (ref, spent, last_seen_run; at most `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 refs `[PLACEHOLDER]`). An encounter ends, and its budget resets, when that horror has been out of the seat's range (not within `HORROR_NEAR_R` 288 px on the same body) for `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` 120 s. One horror therefore contributes at most 3000 mSU per encounter. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED; the E3 design-intent question OQ-SN-45 is settled by best judgment as option (a), which keeps the claim "a horror in the beam lowers sanity" and changes only the accounting: (1) **marginal charging**: the budget is charged with what E3 actually added to the level that run, `delta = net_with_E3 - net_without_E3` (both through the same clamp), shared among the near horrors in ascending ref order, each capped by its own `pay_h`; a horror whose contact the net cap absorbs (BREACH, or a derelict with the cap already binding) therefore spends little or none of its budget; (2) `pay_h` is clamped at 0 (`max(0, min(EXP_HORROR, HORROR_ENC_BUDGET - spent_h))`); (3) **evict-and-retain**: a horror evicted from `horror_enc[]` by the fifth ref leaves a tombstone `(ref, spent, last_seen_run)` that keeps its spent budget until its own `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` elapses, so a five-horror cycle cannot reset budgets by eviction; the tombstones are bounded by the registered `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX`; (4) the farm bound is restated in D.10 from the lowest ambient floor and over 1 to `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` concurrent horrors.)* Per-horror refs are SIM refs (EM-21).
*Why.* *(Orchestrator ruling: additive to R6.)* A repelled horror held at the edge of `HORROR_NEAR_R` would otherwise pay 20 mSU per run indefinitely, an unbounded farm for deliberate self-lowering (the concept's "watch for players lowering their own sanity deliberately"). "Sustained horror contact" in the rulings' calibration sentence therefore reads "repeated horror encounters".
*Edge notes.* Provisional on Horror Entities (Designed, horror-entities.md): it publishes `horror.view` (HE-37) positions and states and computes no exposure (HE-32, HE-40, AC-HE-101); Sanity owns `HORROR_NEAR_R`, `EXP_HORROR` and this budget. The seat's own noise and visibility remain Attention's. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### States and Transitions

Every transition below is evaluated inside the `sanity.drift` run (SN-2) in the fixed order given there, per seat, and is caused by the hook, by one of the two Class B mutators, or by the debug controls. **No player action, no other simulation system, and no presentation system can cause a transition directly**; the player affects the level only through the true world (SN-5, SN-8) and affects a lie only through a falsification or a real repair (SN-17). "Who may cause" is therefore stated as the single caller.

#### Stage machine (per seat; formula D.2)

| State | Entry condition (strictly below / strictly above; `stage_dwell >= STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25 or forced) | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 SANE | initial; or from 1 when `level > 72000` | `level < 70000` | to 1 | hook run (D.2); `force_*` (debug) |
| 1 INSTRUMENTS | from 0 at `level < 70000`; from 2 when `level > 57000` | `level < 55000`, or `level > 72000` | to 2; to 0 | hook run; `force_*` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 2 LIGHT | from 1 at `level < 55000`; from 3 when `level > 27000` | `level < 25000`, or `level > 57000` | to 3; to 1 | hook run; `force_*` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 3 WORLD | from 2 at `level < 25000` | `level > 27000` | to 2 | hook run; `force_*` |

Rules: worsening is evaluated before recovery; the stage moves directly to the target when the dwell has expired (it never steps one at a time unless the level sits in the adjacent band); one transition per run, the deeper applying if two dwell timers would expire together; `stage_dwell` resets to 0 on every change and counts up by one per run, saturating at `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS`; a level exactly on a threshold holds the current stage; a restore re-uses the stored `stage` and `stage_dwell` (no recompute); `force_*` recomputes with the dwell saturated. Reach per stage: SN-16.

#### Victim-tell flag `tell_on` (per seat)

| State | Entry | Exit | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tell_on` = false | initial; `level > 35000` (`SAN_TELL_OFF`) | `level < 30000` (`SAN_TELL_ON`) | hook run |
| `tell_on` = true | `level < 30000` | `level > 35000` | hook run |

No dwell: the flag is a lead indicator, set early. It is read through `sanity_view` by the Filter (the victim-side fringe, class c), Adaptive Audio & Tells (presentation only) and the Debug Overlay (DIAGNOSTIC); no simulation system reads it.

#### Harm gate `harm_eligible` (per seat; formula D.3)

| State | Entry | Exit | Counter `harm_below_runs` | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NOT_ELIGIBLE, counting | a run with `level < 15000` (`SAN_HARM_ENTER`) | any run with `level >= 15000` (counter resets to 0) | +1 per run, up to 150 | hook run |
| ELIGIBLE | `harm_below_runs >= 150` (`SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS`, 30 s) | any run with `level > 20000` (`SAN_HARM_EXIT`): clears at once, counter 0 | frozen at 150 while eligible | hook run; `force_*` (debug) |

A restore landing mid-dwell resumes the persisted counter; the bit does not reset.

#### Lie lifecycle (per ledger entry; SN-16, SN-17)

| State | Entered when | Leaves to | In `sanity.perception`? | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | none | PENDING on an accepted onset draw | no | hook run (D.5) |
| PENDING | D.5 accepts; delay of `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` 10 to 25 runs set | ACTIVE after the delay if the SN-15 re-check passes; INVALIDATED if not; **DEBUNKED** if a `field_check` completes on the subject during the delay *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: SN-17 already debunks "any ACTIVE or PENDING lie", the table lacked the edge)* | no | hook run; `record_falsification` |
| ACTIVE | delay elapsed and re-check ok; **`lies_seen` is incremented on this step** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it was on accept)* | EXPIRED at `end_run`; DEBUNKED; INVALIDATED | yes, with `leak_modes` | hook run |
| EXPIRED | `run >= end_run` | removed; no immunity | no | hook run (the time rung) |
| DEBUNKED | `record_falsification` on the subject (a `field_check` completion only; a repair or inspect completion does not call it, SN-17) | removed; immunity for `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` | no | `record_falsification` (from PENDING or ACTIVE; Interaction's `interaction.step` on the completion tick) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| INVALIDATED | any SN-15 re-check fails (witness ended, atmosphere band left OK, a fixed gauge relied on at activation destroyed, true severity worse than `onset_stage` allows, the seat carrying a module at the activation re-check of a single-instrument-loop lie), the subject is destroyed, salvaged or no longer resolves (including through a restore), the true state now equals the displayed state or the derived `direction` differs from the stored one. A class switched off by data is **not** an INVALIDATED cause: live lies expire normally (SN-28) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)* | removed; no immunity (a destroyed subject cannot be immune) | no | hook run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

A stage change does not end a lie (SN-16). A check that completes on the same hook tick lands before the hook runs (SN-2 `after` edge, SN-17 edge notes, EC-SN-12). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

#### Compartment-entry state `entry_pending` (per seat; SN-16)

| State | Entered when | Left when | Who may cause |
|---|---|---|---|
| clear | initial; the batch window opened; the seat left the compartment | the seat's compartment changes (which also empties `evented[]`, discards any held events and clears `batch_end_run`, SN-16) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)* | hook run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| set | the seat's compartment changed | the first run at which the seat's lit candidate set is non-empty (the batch window opens: `batch_end_run` = run + `ENTRY_BATCH_RUNS`, and the events formed inside it are processed together at its close), or the seat leaves that compartment | hook run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: per-visit events and the batch window, SN-16)* |

#### Seat lifecycle (SN-3)

| State | Drift | Onset | Ledger | Entered by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| UNBOUND | frozen | none | retained | the seat is absent (VS-B) |
| BOUND_ALIVE | D.1 | yes | live | bind |
| DOWNED | D.1 | yes | live | Suit DOWNED |
| DEAD | frozen | none | frozen | Suit DEAD; in the MVP the death stub reloads the last autosave (SL-7), replacing the frozen state with the restored one |

The seat state is read from Suit (DOWNED, DEAD) and the session's binding table; Sanity never sets it.

### Interactions with Other Systems

`(provisional — <dep> undesigned/in flight)` marks a neighbour with no GDD, or one whose GDD is in flight; this GDD is the first writer of its half, and lists only what Sanity supplies or needs (reflexion P-4). Where the neighbour is designed, its own Interactions row is **quoted** so the two documents can be checked against each other. Sibling GDDs are uncommitted in this run, so line references are re-grepped at the registry stage.

#### Command & Simulation Layer (designed; Hard)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Data in | `TICK_RATE` 60; the CR-3 hook order and topological sort; the CR-9 WriteScope; `rng_derive` and `rng_int` (D.10); `restore_reason` (**never consumed** for content, SN-24); the CR-8 `restore_after` order |
| Data out | three blocks (SN-1) and the hook `sanity.drift` (SN-2); the isolation rule that no sim reader reads `sanity.perception` (CR-12, H5) |
| Interface owner and names | Kernel owns CR-3, CR-8, CR-9, CR-12, D.10; Sanity owns the block ids `sanity.state`, `sanity.ledger`, `sanity.perception` and the hook id `sanity.drift` |
| The kernel's own row (KRN:202, row 10) | "Sanity level per player (`SIM`, `PER_PLAYER`, `persist`) and perception output (`PERCEPTION_OUT`, not persisted); drift hook at divisor 12; falsification checks arrive as commands from Interaction/Suit ... Keyed by PlayerId, not peer ID. Friendly-harm gate: Interaction's `validate` calls a Sanity query on `SIM` data (CR-7). A falsification result any sim system depends on must be written to a `SIM` block. **Replication (VS-B)**: only `PERCEPTION_OUT` is `OWNER_PRIVATE`; the sanity-level block is at least `SHARED_SUMMARY` for its threshold flag, because P4(c) requires the *victim* to see the attacker's tell. Sanity also prices how a reason code reaches a hallucinating player (P4 cost rule)" |
| Consistent with | Yes on every clause, with four wording refreshes owed to the kernel: the row lists two blocks (Sanity registers three, SN-1); it omits `persist_scope` (SL OQ-21; stated here as WORLD); the phrase "falsification checks arrive as commands" is realised as a Class B mutator called from Interaction's `interaction.step` hook on the completion tick (SN-17, SN-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*; and CR-12 and the row say perception is "reproducible from sanity level + seed" while it is rebuilt from level and the persisted ledger (C19). The fixture label `sanity (12, 0)` at KRN:787 is an example, not a registration (C6) |

#### Attention System (designed; Hard)

|---|---|
| Data in | `attention.view` fields `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome`, `tell_epoch` (one-way, SN-7). **Not** `level_pm`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X4, escalation freezes in Attention while its level is below `theta_HOLD`, so a held tier keeps paying its own `EXP_TELL[tier]` for as long as it is published; D.1 reads the published `tier` and needs no change. X3, the carry or sortie-burden intake and the recalibrated weights and `theta`, change how soon an ordinary sortie reaches the TELL, not what a TELL costs here; the breach-epoch cost estimates in SN-29 are [UNV] and are re-read when Attention republishes its timings.)* |
| Data out | nothing. Sanity never writes Attention, feeds nothing into its thresholds, and creates no phantom tell and no false calm |
| Interface owner and names | Attention owns the fields (AT-16, ATT:214-230) and the phase machine; Sanity owns `EXP_TELL[tier]`, `EXP_BREACH`, `EXP_SUBSIDING`, `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` |
| Attention's own row (ATT:374) | "Sanity & Hallucination Rules (no file — provisional) | nothing | `level_pm`, `phase` as SIM reads | KRN:253 (its drift hook at divisor 12) | **Must repeat:** one-way (Sanity reads Attention, never writes it); no feedback into thresholds; it states whether Attention events change sanity and by how much (Sanity's rule); the tell is a truth channel and Sanity creates no phantom tell and no false calm at any tier (closed by pillar at the gate, AT-17); **must consider:** phantom entities that co-occur with real tells and breaches, where they are hardest to separate; Sanity audio uses a family disjoint from the tell's" |
| Consistent with | Every must-repeat is carried: one-way (SN-7), no feedback, the exposure rule (SN-7, the answer to "by how much"), truth channel (SN-7, SN-22), co-occurrence (SN-22), disjoint audio family (SN-25). **One divergence, routed (C11):** Attention's row says Sanity reads `level_pm` and `phase`; Sanity reads `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch` and does **not** read `level_pm`. All four are fields of the public SIM view, so the reader notes of `tier` and `tell_outcome` (which name Lighting and Audio, not Sanity) should add Sanity (`phase` already names Sanity and `tell_epoch` is read by 'all'), and the row should drop `level_pm` |
| Must repeat from Attention | `web_strand_*`, `vfx_tell_*` and the band slide are exclusive to Attention's presenter and never reused (AV-18); Sanity's fringe is one continuous band tangent to the cone rim, no root, breathing 4 to 6 s, per viewer, not locked to `tell_beat`, never in a falsely extended reach (ATT:93, AC-AT-109). Sanity supplies the AC-AT-73 double (a Sanity double that raises a phantom, a false "hull failing" state, a phantom emitter and phantom loot while `phase` is CALM leaves the view, `emitters()` and `TellAudit` unchanged) |

## Formulas

*Conventions (read first; every formula below uses them).* **Units.** The level is in **mSU** (integer); one **run** is one `sanity.drift` invocation (`SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` 12 ticks = 0.2 s, 5 runs per second); rates are mSU per run; durations in seconds are `runs / 5`. `clamp(x, lo, hi) = max(lo, min(hi, x))`; `div` is floor division and is applied to non-negative operands only; `ceil(a / b)` for `a >= 0` is `(a + b - 1) div b`. **Integer rule (kernel determinism).** Everything that is hashed, persisted or replicated is integer: D.1 to D.6 and D.8 to D.10 are integer and run inside the hook or at boot. D.7 and D.11 to D.13 are **design-time and QA formulas**: they are seconds-valued or fractional because nothing in them is hashed, persisted or replicated, they never run in the sim, and a float appears only in them and in the test oracles. **Placeholders used in the worked examples** (all `[PLACEHOLDER]`, tuned in `assets/data/sanity/`): `SAN_MAX` 100000, `STAGE_ENTER[1..3]` 70000 / 55000 / 25000, `STAGE_HYST` 2000, `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` 25, `SAN_TELL_ON` 30000, `SAN_TELL_OFF` 35000, `SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000, `SAN_HARM_EXIT` 20000, `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` 150, `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36, `REC_SETTLED` 12, `REC_IDLE` 1, `REC_CREW` 3, `REC_MAX` 15, `PENDING_MAX` 3000, `RING_EXPOSURE[1..3]` 0 / 4 / 8, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 20, `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1..3]` 53000 / 52000 / 43000, `EXP_TELL[1..3]` 6 / 12 / 20, `EXP_BREACH` 28, `EXP_SUBSIDING` 14, `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500, `EXP_HORROR` 20 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the stage-2 entry is 55000, was 45000, and the ring-1 floor is 53000, was 62000, per OQ-SN-38; see D.8)*. **Cited, never re-derived:** KRN D.10 (`rng_derive`, `unit`, `rng_int`), the Graph's `HP_OK` 7000 and `HP_FAIL` 3000, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` 204, `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000, `V_WALK` 2 px per tick, `TILE_PX` 48, `REACH_PX` 72, `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk 1 / run 4 / carry 1) and `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000 AU, `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` 300, `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000. **Evaluation order inside one run** is SN-2 steps (1) to (7): inputs, D.1, D.2 and D.3, ledger upkeep, lit-entry events and D.5, perception rebuild, the visit bookkeeping (`evented[]`, `batch_end_run`). Hash outputs in the D.5 and D.6 examples are illustrative until the kernel's `mix64` ADR picks the function (as KRN D.10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

#### D.1 `sanity_drift_step`

The `sanity_drift_step` formula is defined as:

`E1 = (level > AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]) ? RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + (aboard ? ABOARD_EXPOSURE : 0) : 0` (in the pod, `ring` is Attention's `held_ring` and `aboard` is false, SN-5 E1); `E2 = {CALM: 0, TELL: EXP_TELL[tier], BREACH: EXP_BREACH, SUBSIDING: EXP_SUBSIDING}[phase]`; `E3 = sum over near horrors h of max(0, min(EXP_HORROR, HORROR_ENC_BUDGET - spent_h))` (the budget is then charged with the marginal contribution, D.10); `E = E1 + E2 + E3 + E4`; `R = (settled ? REC_SETTLED : REC_IDLE) + (crew_saner ? REC_CREW : 0)`; `base = E - R`; `draw = min(pending, max(0, DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN - base))`; `net = clamp(base + draw, -REC_MAX, DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN)`; `level' = clamp(level - net, 0, SAN_MAX)`; `pending' = min(PENDING_MAX, pending - draw + startle_add)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `level` | int mSU | 0 to `SAN_MAX` | `sanity.state` | the seat's level at the start of the run; the only state this formula lowers |
| `ring` | int | 1 to 3 | `ring(PlayerId)` = `ring_of(actor_section(actor))`, AT-6, WM-27 *(Web Map & Transit, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*; Attention's `held_ring` while the seat is IN_POD | depth ring of the seat's section; unknown resolves to 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `aboard` | bool | - | `not body_is_home` and `loco_state` is not IN_POD (WM-27, OQ-WM-6) *(Web Map & Transit, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the seat's body is a foreign body; false in the pod, so a pod hop is never charged `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `RING_EXPOSURE[ring]` | int mSU/run | 0 / 4 / 8 | knob | depth term of E1 |
| `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` | int mSU/run | 20 (safe 8 to 20) | knob | foreign-body term of E1 |
| `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` | int mSU | 53000 / 52000 / 43000 | knob (SN-29; ring-1 floor lowered by the user ruling on OQ-SN-38, D.8) | E1 contributes only while `level` is strictly above it; E2 and E3 are never floored *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `phase`, `tier` | enum, int | CALM/TELL/BREACH/SUBSIDING; 0 to 3 | `attention.view` (AT-16), read one-way | `tier` 0 in TELL contributes 0 |
| `EXP_TELL[tier]` | int mSU/run | 6 / 12 / 20 for tier 1 to 3 | knob | tell-phase rate |
| `EXP_BREACH`, `EXP_SUBSIDING` | int mSU/run | 28, 14 | knob | breach and subsiding rates |
| `near horror h`, `spent_h` | set, int mSU | up to `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 refs; 0 to `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 | `horror.view` positions (HE-37), `horror_enc[]` *(Horror Entities, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)* | horror within `HORROR_NEAR_R` on the same body, and its encounter spend (D.10) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `E4` | int | 0 | Co-op (dormant) | witnessed crew death; 0 until VS-B |
| `settled` | bool | - | Body (`body_is_home`), Graph bands of the seat's compartment, Attention `phase`/`tier` | home body AND all three atmosphere bands OK AND `phase` CALM AND `tier` 0 (SN-8) |
| `crew_saner` | bool | - | Co-op (dormant) | another bound seat within `CREW_R` is at least one stage saner |
| `REC_SETTLED`, `REC_IDLE`, `REC_CREW` | int mSU/run | 12, 1, 3 | knobs | recovery terms |
| `REC_MAX` | int mSU/run | 15 | derived: `REC_SETTLED + REC_CREW` | lower clamp of `net`; unreachable by construction, kept as a build assertion |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` | int mSU/run | 36 (<= 36 per D.4) | knob | the net cap |
| `pending` | int mSU | 0 to `PENDING_MAX` 3000 | `sanity.state` | undrained startle debt |
| `startle_add` | int mSU | 0 or `EXP_BREACH_ENTRY` 1500 | the CALM run where `tell_epoch` differs from `last_tell_epoch` and `tell_outcome` is BREACHED (`last_tell_epoch` is overwritten on every such CALM run, SN-7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)* | the only way `pending` grows |
| `net` | int mSU/run | -15 to +36 | result | positive means loss |

**Output Range:** `net` is -15 (settled home, saner crewmate, `E` = 0) to +36 (any `base + draw` at or above 36). `level'` is in `[0, SAN_MAX]` and `pending'` in `[0, PENDING_MAX]`. `E` can reach 76 with one horror near (`8 + 20 + 28 + 20`), so the cap, not the sum, is the binding bound, and the lower clamp never binds (`E >= 0`, `R <= 15`). Nothing outside D.1 writes `level` except the debug `force_level` (SN-9). The drain is `draw`, which can exceed the visible level change when `base` is negative (the settled-home startle repays faster than it hurts); a zero `pending` makes `draw` 0.
**Example:** (all at `phase`/`tier` shown, `pending` 0 unless stated)
- A. Ring 1 derelict, TELL tier 2, `level` 80000: `E1 = 0 + 20 = 20`, `E2 = 12`, `E = 32`, `R = 1` (aboard, not settled), `base = 31`, `draw = 0`, `net = 31`; `level'` = 79969.
- B. The same at `level` 52000, below the ring-1 floor 53000: `E1 = 0`, `E = 12`, `base = 11`, `net = 11`; `level'` = 51989. The floor removes only the ambient term. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- C. Ring 3 derelict, BREACH, one horror near, `pending` 1500, `level` 80000: `E1 = 8 + 20 = 28`, `E2 = 28`, `E3 = 20`, `E = 76`, `R = 1`, `base = 75`, `draw = min(1500, max(0, 36 - 75)) = 0`, `net = clamp(75, -15, 36) = 36`; `level'` = 79964, `pending'` 1500 (the cap binds and the startle waits).
- D. Home body, ring 1, settled, `level` 50000: `E = 0`, `R = 12`, `base = -12`, `net = -12`; `level'` = 50012.
- E. Home body, ring 1, the first CALM run after the breach (SUBSIDING has just ended, `tell_outcome` BREACHED, `tell_epoch` differs from `last_tell_epoch`), `level` 90000, `pending` 0, settled (bands OK, tier 0): `E1 = 0`, `E2 = 0` (CALM), `E = 0`, `R = 12`, `base = -12`, `draw = 0` (`pending` is read before the credit), `net = -12`; `level'` = 90012, `pending'` = `startle_add` = 1500, `last_tell_epoch` overwritten. Then each later run: `draw = min(1500, max(0, 36 - (-12))) = 48`, `net = -12 + 48 = 36`; runs 1 to 31 after the credit run draw 48 each (1488 mSU), `pending` is 12, and run 32 draws 12 (`net` 0) and clears it: the debt is repaid in 32 runs = 6.4 s. Away from home (a ring-1 derelict, `level` above the floor, CALM): `E = 20`, `R = 1`, `base = 19`, `draw = min(pending, 17)`, 88 runs of 17 (1496) and a last run of 4, 89 runs = 17.8 s. The earlier arithmetic here (credit at BREACH entry, `draw` 9, 167 runs) assumed a breach-entry epoch Attention does not publish and is removed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED, closes OQ-SN-42.)*
- F. Ring 1 derelict, BREACH, `pending` 1500, `level` 80000: `E = 20 + 28 = 48`, `base = 47`, `draw = 0`, `net = 36`; the startle waits until BREACH ends.
- G. Home body ring 1, SUBSIDING, `pending` 1500: `E = 14`, `R = 1`, `base = 13`, `draw = min(1500, 23) = 23`, `net = 36`; the debt clears in `ceil(1500 / 23)` = 66 runs = 13.2 s.
- H. Home body, settled, saner crewmate within `CREW_R` (VS-B): `E = 0`, `R = 15`, `net = -15`; `level'` = 50015.
- I. Seat IN_POD with `held_ring` 1, phase CALM, `level` 80000: `aboard` false, `E1 = 0`, `E = 0`, `settled` false so `R = REC_IDLE` = 1, `base = -1`, `net = -1`; `level'` = 80001. The same seat docked aboard derelict A: `E1 = 20`, `base = 19`, `net = 19`.

#### D.2 `stage_of`

The `stage_of` formula is defined as:

`stage' = stage_of(level, stage, stage_dwell)`: return `stage` while `stage_dwell < STAGE_DWELL_RUNS`; otherwise step `target` deeper while `level < STAGE_ENTER[target + 1]` (worsening is evaluated first), and only if it did not move, step `target` shallower while `level > STAGE_ENTER[target] + STAGE_HYST`

The same rule as a step list (one transition per run, the deeper applying):

```
if stage_dwell < STAGE_DWELL_RUNS: return stage
target = stage
while target < 3 and level < STAGE_ENTER[target + 1]: target += 1      # worsen first
if target == stage:
    while target > 0 and level > STAGE_ENTER[target] + STAGE_HYST: target -= 1
return target                                                          # caller resets stage_dwell to 0 on a change
```

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `level` | int mSU | 0 to 100000 | `sanity.state` | level after D.1 |
| `stage` | int | 0 to 3 | `sanity.state` | stage before this run |
| `stage_dwell` | int runs | 0 to `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` (saturating) | `sanity.state` | runs since the last change; +1 per run |
| `STAGE_ENTER[s]` | int mSU | 70000 / 55000 / 25000 for s = 1..3 | knob | entry thresholds, strictly below; stage 2 raised from 45000 by the user ruling on OQ-SN-38 (D.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `STAGE_HYST` | int mSU | 2000 (safe 1000 to 2000; D.8 (b) caps it at 2000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)* | knob | the exit threshold of stage `s` is `STAGE_ENTER[s] + STAGE_HYST` (72000 / 57000 / 27000), strictly above *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `STAGE_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 25 (5 s; safe 10 to 50) | knob | minimum runs between changes |

**Output Range:** an integer in 0 to 3; a stage 4 is impossible by construction. Entry is strictly below and exit strictly above, so `level` exactly 70000 stays stage 0 and exactly 72000 stays stage 1. Two thresholds cannot be crossed inside one dwell under the cap (`25 x 36` = 900 mSU against gaps of at least 15000, the 70000-to-55000 gap, after the OQ-SN-38 ruling); the only ways to see a jump of more than one stage are a restore (the stored stage is kept, no recompute) and a debug `force_*` (recompute with the dwell saturated, directly to the target).
**Example:** `level` 69964, `stage` 0, `stage_dwell` 25: 69964 < 70000 so `target` 1; 69964 is not < 55000, so stage 1 and the dwell resets. At 71000 the stage holds (71000 is not above 72000). At 72001 with the dwell back at 25, `target` 0. Direct drop: `stage` 2, `level` 57500, dwell 25: not worsening; 57500 > 57000 so `target` 1; 57500 is not > 72000 so it stops at 1. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

#### D.3 `harm_gate`

The `harm_gate` formula is defined as:

`harm_below_runs' = (level < SAN_HARM_ENTER) ? min(SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS, harm_below_runs + 1) : 0` while not eligible; `harm_eligible' = harm_eligible ? (level <= SAN_HARM_EXIT) : (harm_below_runs' >= SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS)`; `tell_on' = (level < SAN_TELL_ON) ? true : (level > SAN_TELL_OFF) ? false : tell_on`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SAN_HARM_ENTER` | int mSU | 15000 | knob | harm-gate entry threshold, strictly below |
| `SAN_HARM_EXIT` | int mSU | 20000 (> `SAN_HARM_ENTER`; safe 15612, the D.9 boot assertion, to 25000) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* | knob | exit threshold, strictly above, clears at once |
| `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 150 (30 s; safe 122 to 300, D.4 (v)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)* | knob | consecutive runs below `SAN_HARM_ENTER` needed |
| `harm_below_runs` | int runs | 0 to 150 | `sanity.state` | dwell counter; frozen at 150 while eligible and reset to 0 on clearing |
| `SAN_TELL_ON`, `SAN_TELL_OFF` | int mSU | 30000, 35000 (`SAN_TELL_OFF` > `SAN_TELL_ON`) | knobs | victim-tell flag thresholds; no dwell |
| `harm_eligible`, `tell_on` | bool | - | `sanity.state` | the two flags; `harm_eligible` is the single SIM bit the gate query and the class-(c) render read (SN-11) |

**Output Range:** two booleans and a counter in 0 to 150. The earliest eligibility is 150 runs = 30 s after the first run below 15000. Outside debug force: `harm_eligible` implies `stage == 3` (eligibility needs the level below 20000 and stage 3 is held until 27000) and implies `tell_on` (cleared only above 35000), asserted at boot as corollaries of D.4.
**Example:** the level crosses 14990 at run n: counter 1; at run n + 149 the counter reaches 150 and the bit sets (30 s). If instead the level returns to exactly 15000 at run n + 60 (counter 60), the counter resets to 0 (a run at or above `SAN_HARM_ENTER` resets). Once eligible at 9600, recovery to 20001 clears the bit that run; at 20000 exactly it holds. `tell_on` sets at 29999 and, at 33000, stays set (inside the band).

#### D.4 `drift_lead_invariant`

The `drift_lead_invariant` formula is defined as:

`lead_runs(a, b) = (a - b + DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN - 1) div DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`; `lead_s(a, b) = lead_runs(a, b) x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR / TICK_RATE`; build assertions (i) `lead_s(STAGE_ENTER[1], SAN_TELL_ON) >= SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S`, (ii) `lead_s(SAN_TELL_ON, SAN_HARM_ENTER) >= SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, (iii) `lead_s(STAGE_ENTER[1], SAN_HARM_ENTER) >= SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S`, (iv) `lead_s(SAN_TELL_ON, SAN_HARM_ENTER) + SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR / TICK_RATE >= SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, **(v) `lead_s(min(AMBIENT_FLOOR), SAN_HARM_ENTER) + SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR / TICK_RATE >= SAN_FLOOR_TO_HARM_MIN_S`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: (v) is the lead measured from the lowest ambient floor, where routine play starts)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `a`, `b` | int mSU | 0 to 100000 | knobs (thresholds) | upper and lower level of a bound |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` | int mSU/run | 36 | knob | the net cap, the worst-case descent rate |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | int ticks | 12 | equals the kernel divisor by relation (KRN:54, KRN:202); not a knob | ticks per run |
| `SAN_STAGE1_TO_TELL_MIN_S` | int s | 220 | knob | required stage-1 to `tell_on` lead |
| `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S` | int s | 60 (safe 45 to 83, one knob at a time, others at default) | knob | required `tell_on` to eligibility lead |
| `SAN_MIN_ONSET_TO_HARM_S` | int s | 300 (safe 240 to 305) | knob | required stage-1 entry to eligibility lead |
| `SAN_FLOOR_TO_HARM_MIN_S` | int s | 180 `[PLACEHOLDER]` value; the target is confirmed by the user ruling on OQ-SN-43 (2026-10-27: three minutes from routine ring-3 play to lethal friendly harm); safe 150 to 185 with the other knobs at their defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: upper end was 240)* | knob *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: new, registry request)* | required lead from the lowest ambient floor to eligibility, including the dwell |
| `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR)` | int mSU | 43000 | knob (SN-29) | the `a` of bound (v) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `STAGE_ENTER[1]`, `SAN_TELL_ON`, `SAN_HARM_ENTER` | int mSU | 70000, 30000, 15000 | knobs | the `a` and `b` of bounds (i) to (iv) |
| `SAN_HARM_DWELL_RUNS` | int runs | 150 | knob | the dwell added in bound (iv) |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per bound. At the defaults:

| Bound | Distance (mSU) | Runs at cap 36 | Seconds | Required | Margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (i) 70000 to 30000 | 40000 | 1112 | 222.4 | 220 | 2.4 s |
| (ii) 30000 to 15000 | 15000 | 417 | 83.4 | 60 | 23.4 s |
| (iii) 70000 to 15000 | 55000 | 1528 | 305.6 (the bit sets on run 1677 = 335.4 s with the dwell, SN-10) | 300 | 5.6 s |
| (iv) `SAN_TELL_ON` to eligible, with the 150-run dwell | 15000 | 417 + 150 = 567 | 113.4 | 60 | 53.4 s |
| (v) lowest floor 43000 to eligible, with the dwell *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 28000 | 778 + 150 = 928 | 185.6 | 180 | 5.6 s |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* The same lead from the other floors, for reading only and not asserted: ring 2 (52000) 37000 mSU = 1028 + 150 runs = 235.6 s; ring 1 (53000) 38000 mSU = 1056 + 150 runs = 241.2 s (was 291.2 s from the 62000 floor before the OQ-SN-38 ruling). The lowest-floor case binds, so asserting it covers the others. **Re-run for the OQ-SN-38 pair** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: bounds (i) to (v) read only `STAGE_ENTER[1]` 70000, `SAN_TELL_ON` 30000, `SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000, `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR)` 43000, the cap 36 and the dwell; none of them moved (the stage-2 entry and the ring-1 floor are not inputs of any bound), so every row of the table above and every margin (2.4, 23.4, 5.6, 53.4 and 5.6 s) is unchanged and all five pass. Closed form for (v): `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN < (min floor - SAN_HARM_ENTER) / (5 x (S_v - 30) - 1)` = `28000 / 749` = 37.4 with `S_v` 180, which the cap 36 passes, and `SAN_HARM_ENTER <= min floor - (749 x 36 + 1)` = 43000 - 26965 = 16035 (the same ceiling as bound (iii)).

**Closed form for the cap.** Bound `i` needs `lead_runs >= 5 x S_i`, which holds exactly when `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN < d_i / (5 x S_i - 1)`: (i) `40000 / 1099` = 36.4, (ii) `15000 / 299` = 50.2, (iii) `55000 / 1499` = 36.7, so the largest passing cap is `min(36, 50, 36)` = 36 and 36 is the value used. At 37: (i) `ceil(40000 / 37)` = 1082 runs = 216.4 s fails, and (iii) `ceil(55000 / 37)` = 1487 runs = 297.4 s fails. (i) and (iii) bind together. **Cap-is-binding demonstration:** the uncapped `E - R` exceeds 36 in every danger state that matters: ring 3 aboard in BREACH with a horror near is `8 + 20 + 28 + 20 - 1` = 75; without the horror `8 + 20 + 28 - 1` = 55; ring 1 aboard in BREACH `0 + 20 + 28 - 1` = 47; ring 1 aboard in a tier-3 TELL `0 + 20 + 20 - 1` = 39; a tier-2 TELL aboard (`0 + 20 + 12 - 1` = 31) is the first state below the cap. **Example:** raise the cap to 40 with nothing else changed: (iii) gives `ceil(55000 / 40)` = 1375 runs = 275.0 s against 300 s, failing; the boot assertion refuses with `SANITY_BOOT_LEAD_INVARIANT`. Any retune of a threshold, the cap, the divisor, an exposure rate or an ambient floor re-runs all five (reflexion P-2, P-7). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*

#### D.5 `lie_onset`

The `lie_onset` formula is defined as:

`w = (u in repaired_recent within REPAIR_BIAS_S) ? REPAIR_BIAS_W : 1`; `p_pm = min(1000, ONSET_PM[s] x w)`; `x = rng_int(1, 1000)` at `c`; accept iff `x <= p_pm`; on accept `kind = weighted pick over the legal kinds by KIND_W[kind][s]` via `rng_int(1, sum_W)` at `c + 1`, `life_runs = rng_int(5 x 40, 5 x 120)` at `c + 2`, `delay_runs = rng_int(10, 25)` at `c + 3`, `visit[p][u] = c + 4`, `last_onset_run = run`, `lie_id = next_lie_id` then `next_lie_id += 1`, `slot` = the lowest free slot (SN-23; none of the last three draws or advances a counter); on reject `visit[p][u] = c + 1`

It is applied to each gated lit-entry event on subject `u` for seat `p` at stage `s`, in the SN-16 order, with `c = visit[p][u]` on the stream `"sanity.lie/<p>/<u>"`. At the MVP `repaired_recent[]` is always empty, so `w = 1` for every `u` regardless of `REPAIR_BIAS_W`; the formula is unchanged (G4) and its `REPAIR_BIAS_W` above 1 branch is exercised only by the seeded fixture of AC-SN-133 and AC-SN-59 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G2)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: **preconditions.** Before the first draw the caller computes the legal-kind set `K(p, u, s)` (SN-16: true state, L-D direction rails, surface kind, stage reach, `has_both_frames`, first-lie restriction) and `sum_W = sum over k in K of KIND_W[k][s]`. If `sum_W == 0` the event is refused at the pre-draw gate: no draw, no counter advance, `visit[p][u]` unchanged. `sum_W >= 1` is therefore a precondition of the `rng_int(1, sum_W)` call at `c + 1`; D.5 is never entered with an empty set. The accepted kind's `direction` (L-D) is stored on the entry. The accept draw and the pick use the same `K`, computed once.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `s` | int | 1 to 3 | `sanity.state` stage | stage 0 never reaches this formula (pre-draw gate) |
| `ONSET_PM[s]` | int permille | 150 / 300 / 550 (safe 50 to 800) | knob | accept chance of one gated event |
| `REPAIR_BIAS_S`, `REPAIR_BIAS_W` | int s, int | 60, 1 (safe 0 to 120; 1 to 3; `REPAIR_BIAS_W` 1 is the MVP default and disables the bias, user ruling on OQ-SN-40) | knobs | with `W` above 1 (post-MVP only), a subject repaired within the window is `W` times as likely to come undone; at the MVP default `w` is always 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `visit[p][u]` | int | 0 to 2^53 | `sanity.ledger` map, pruned on subject destruction | counts only gated events on `u` for `p` (a failed gate draws nothing and advances nothing) |
| `KIND_W[kind][s]` | int | stage 1 = 45 / 25 / 30 / 0; stage 2 = 40 / 25 / 30 / 5; stage 3 = 25 / 30 / 15 / 30 for FALSE_OK / FALSE_FAIL / GLIDE / SPRITE_SWAP | knobs | weights over the legal-kind set `K` only, which the subject's true state, direction rails, surface kind, reach and first-lie status define (SN-16, L-D); an empty `K` never reaches the pick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `LIE_LIFE_S` | int s | 40 to 120 (safe 20 to 180) | knob | the two ends of the life band; times 5 for runs |
| `ONSET_DELAY_RUNS` | int runs | 10 to 25 (safe 5 to 50) | knob | invisible PENDING delay before re-validation |
| `rng_int(lo, hi)` | int | inclusive, no modulo bias | KRN D.10 | on the stream key above, counter as shown |

**Output Range:** `p_pm` in 150 to 550 at the MVP default `REPAIR_BIAS_W` 1 (1000 is reachable only if the bias is re-enabled, at stage 3 with weight 2: `min(1000, 1100)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; `life_runs` 200 to 600 (40 to 120 s); `delay_runs` 10 to 25 (2 to 5 s). Draw consumption is exactly 0 (a pre-draw gate refused, including `sum_W == 0`), 1 (reject) or 4 (accept) per event, so a replay is bit-exact (SL H2, G4), and `visit[p][u]` is a function of that seat's own actions on that subject only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** stage 2 (`ONSET_PM` 300), a degraded gauge not recently repaired, third gated event on it (`visit` = 2), illustrative `x = 217` at counter 2: 217 <= 300 accept. Legal kinds for a degraded gauge at stage 2 are FALSE_OK 40 and GLIDE 30 (FALSE_FAIL needs a healthy subject, SPRITE_SWAP needs a glow sprite), `sum_W` 70; the roll at counter 3 is 25, so FALSE_OK. Counter 4: `rng_int(200, 600)` = 372 runs = 74.4 s. Counter 5: `rng_int(10, 25)` = 17 runs = 3.4 s. `visit` becomes 6. The same subject repaired 30 s earlier: at the MVP default `REPAIR_BIAS_W` 1, `p_pm` = 300, identical to a subject never repaired, and x = 450 rejects. With the bias re-enabled (`REPAIR_BIAS_W` 2, post-MVP only): `p_pm = min(1000, 300 x 2)` = 600, x = 217 still accepts and x = 450 would now accept where it rejected before; stage 3 with weight 2 and any x in 1 to 1000 accepts. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* **Empty-set examples** (zero draws, `visit` unchanged, no counter moves): a FAILED gauge at stage 1 (FALSE_OK and GLIDE toward health are refused by L-S, FALSE_FAIL needs a healthy subject, SPRITE_SWAP has weight 0); a single-instrument-loop gauge for a seat that is carrying a module (L-2I leaves no credited instrument); a DEGRADED subject on `lethal_fault_modes` at stage 1 (the comfort kinds are refused by L-S, FALSE_FAIL needs healthy, SPRITE_SWAP has weight 0 at stage 1); a machine-sprite surface at stage 3 whose sheet lacks the opposite frame (SPRITE_SWAP is its only kind); and a first-lie event while every lit subject carries a fault or a forced mode.

#### D.6 `lure_dressing` *(class d; VS-A; MVP ships none)*

The `lure_dressing` formula is defined as:

`candidates(j) = sorted by band_id { b in lure_bands : class(b.mid_g) != weight_class(m_j) AND |m_j - b.mid_g| x 1000 >= WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM x b.mid_g }`; `band(j) = candidates(j)[rng_int(0, |candidates(j)| - 1)]` on the stream `"sanity.lure/<PlayerId>"` at `c_lure`, then `c_lure' = c_lure + 1`; the result is stored in `lures[]` subject to `|lures[]| <= LURES_MAX[s]` and `|lures[]| <= (LURE_FRACTION_MAX_PM x n_junk_lit) div 1000`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `m_j` | int g | > 0 | Item DB `instance_mass_g` | true mass of the junk instance |
| `b.mid_g` | int g | > 0 | `assets/data/sanity/lure_bands.json` (Sanity-owned) | the mass the lure pretends to have |
| `weight_class` | enum | LIGHT / MEDIUM / HEAVY | Item DB; `WEIGHT_MEDIUM_G` 5000, `WEIGHT_HEAVY_G` 25000 | class of a mass |
| `WEIGHT_TELL_MIN_PM` | int permille of the lure mass | 300 | cited | smallest gap the breath-step can show |
| `LURES_MAX[s]` | int | 0 / 2 / 3 | knob | per seat, by stage |
| `LURE_FRACTION_MAX_PM` | int permille | 400 | knob | cap on the share of lit junk that may be dressed |
| `n_junk_lit` | int | 0+ | Emitter `lit` over junk in the seat's compartment | junk in the seat's lit set |
| `c_lure` | int | 0 to 2^53 | `sanity.ledger` | advances by 1 per dressing draw, on pickup or lit-entry only |

**Output Range:** a band id, or none when `candidates` is empty (the junk stays undressed: a lie with no weight witness is never created). Real valuables are never dressed as junk (anti-P5); a seat whose `cue_load` is HEAVY through stowed items gets no lure (SE OQ-8).
**Example:** junk 2000 g (LIGHT). Band "plate" `mid_g` 30000 (HEAVY): classes differ and `|2000 - 30000| x 1000` = 28,000,000 >= `300 x 30000` = 9,000,000, a candidate. Band "cell" 2600 (LIGHT): same class, excluded. Band "crate" 6000 (MEDIUM): `4,000 x 1000` = 4,000,000 >= `300 x 6000` = 1,800,000, a candidate. Junk 4800 g against band 5200 (MEDIUM): classes differ but `400 x 1000` = 400,000 < `300 x 5200` = 1,560,000, excluded (the breath-step could not show it). With two candidates the draw `rng_int(0, 1)` picks one; with 5 lit junk items, at most `(400 x 5) div 1000` = 2 are dressed.

#### D.7 `verification_cost_ladder` *(design-time; seconds-valued; every scan cost is a lower bound)*

The `verification_cost_ladder` formula is defined as:

`T_room = ceil(d_room / V_WALK) + n_cross x T_CROSS_PROV + T_look`; `T_second_fixed = ceil(d_second / V_WALK) + n_cross2 x T_CROSS_PROV + T_look`; `T_second_suit = 0` (credited for ALARM-direction lies only, L-2I) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: crossing term added; `n_cross` is the compartment boundaries between the seat and the witness, 0 to `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX`)*; `T_scan = ceil(d_reach / V_WALK) + T_EQUIP + FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`; `T_time = (end_run - run) x SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR`; seconds = `ticks / TICK_RATE`; break-even `d*(n) = ((FIELD_CHECK_TICKS + T_EQUIP - n x T_CROSS_PROV) / TICK_RATE) x (V_WALK x TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)` tiles for a witness `n` compartment boundaries away (`n` = 0 gives the first draft's `(FIELD_CHECK_TICKS / TICK_RATE) x (V_WALK x TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: T_EQUIP and the crossing term are explicit; reach cancels because both options need the player within `REACH_PX` of a surface)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d_room` | int px | 0+ | Layout (the examples below are illustrative placements, not the table of record) | walk to the witness anchor |
| `d_second` | int px | 0+ | Layout | walk to another fixed gauge on the loop |
| `d_reach` | int px | 0 to a few `REACH_PX` | Layout, `REACH_PX` 72 (cited) | walk still needed to bring the lying surface within reach before a scan |
| `V_WALK` | int px/tick | 2 (cited) | pixel pipeline | walking speed: 120 px/s = 2.5 tiles/s at `TILE_PX` 48 |
| `T_look` | int ticks | Interaction's inspect or listen channel | Interaction | read-out time; 0 for a listened witness; carried as 0 in the lower bounds |
| `T_EQUIP` | int ticks | **0 by rule (Suit SE-11: sim swap duration exactly 0 ticks; `equip_tool` is `BUSY` while carrying)** | Suit SE-11 | swap to scanner slot 3; OQ-SN-6 closed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it was "0 until Suit registers a duration")*; scan costs below remain **lower bounds** because of the look, the crossings and the press latency |
| `T_CROSS_PROV` | int ticks | 30 `[PLACEHOLDER]`, provisional, design-time only | Sanity's own assumption until Layout and Interaction state a door or hatch crossing cost (`DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` 30 is Interaction's closest registered figure) | one compartment-boundary crossing on a walk to a witness or a second gauge *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the first draft priced a two-hop witness walk with no crossing cost)* |
| `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` | int steps | 100 (10 s); Graph range 50 to 200 | Life-Support Graph (cited, never re-valued) | the groan cadence that bounds fault 8's rung 1: up to `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS x GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` = 600 ticks = 10 s (20 s at the top of the Graph's range) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (cited; Interaction's range 90 to 240, floor `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` 90) | Interaction | the check channel *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the range read 60 to 240, below the registered floor)* |
| `end_run`, `run` | int | - | `sanity.ledger` | the lie's expiry run and the current run; the time rung |

**Output Range:** non-negative ticks; every lie has at least two finite non-time rungs (L-W guarantees rung 1, L-2I rung 2) and the time rung is at most `5 x 120` runs x 12 = 7200 ticks = 120 s. **Break-even:** `d*(0)` = 2 s x (2 x 60 / 48) = 2 s x 2.5 tiles/s = **5 tiles** one way (witness nearer than 5 tiles: look; farther: scan); at the 90-tick floor `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` it is 3.75 tiles, at 240 ticks 10 tiles. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the "60-tick lower bound, 2.5 tiles" figure is dropped; Interaction's range is 90 to 240.)* **With crossings** (`T_CROSS_PROV` 30 ticks = 0.5 s): `d*(1)` = 1.5 s x 2.5 = **3.75 tiles** and `d*(2)` = 1.0 s x 2.5 = **2.5 tiles**: every compartment boundary between the seat and the witness moves the break-even toward the scan. **Scan from where one stands, lower bound:** `T_scan = ceil(72 / 2) + 0 + 120` = 156 ticks = **2.6 s** (`T_EQUIP` is 0 by SE-11; the figure stays a lower bound for the press latency, the TOOL noise it pays and the lit-channel it needs). A seat that is **carrying** cannot scan at all (`equip_tool` `BUSY`, L-2I) and its rung-2 cost is the drop plus a re-equip.

**The eight MVP faults** (rung 1 distances illustrative; seconds = ticks / 60; a fixed gauge elsewhere costs `ceil(960 / 2)` = 480 ticks = 8.0 s of walk plus `T_look`):

| # | Fault | The lie (module-body only) | Rung 1, the room: witness, example d_room | Lit? | Noise | Rung 2, cheapest honest second instrument (lower bound) | Rung 4, time | Rung 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | O2 pinhole at a clamp | duct or scrubber gauge, state glyph | HISS plus AIRFLOW at the duct, 288 px = 144 ticks = 2.4 s | hear no, see yes | walk 1/tick | scanner 2.6 s; another O2 gauge 8.0 s + look; `suit.o2` at 0 s (current compartment) **only for an ALARM-direction lie**, since for the staged comfort lie it reads the seat's own OK compartment and agrees with it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING)* | <= 120 s | trust, VS-B |
| 2 | O2 seam leak after a weld | pressure gauge, hatch glyph | SEAL_STRAIN creak and rim frost, 192 px = 96 ticks = 1.6 s | no | walk | scanner 2.6 s; gauge 8.0 s; `suit.o2` 0 s only for an ALARM-direction lie. **A comfort lie on this fault is stage 2 or deeper (`LETHAL_FAULT_MIN_STAGE`): reachable in play after the OQ-SN-38 ruling (a long ring-1 sortie, a ring-2 or ring-3 sortie of 391.4 s or 333.4 s, or breach epochs, SN-29)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 3 | Bus overload PWR-OVL | breaker lamp (never a seam's own breaker, SN-15 L-C), cable gauge | cable scorch, arc POP, `served_pm` lamp brightness down the branch, 480 px = 240 ticks = 4.0 s | yes | walk | another fixed gauge on the bus 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s (no Suit key measures POWER) | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 4 | Broken breaker | breaker lamp shows closed (never a seam's own breaker, SN-15 L-C) | dark branch fixtures, relay clunk, 384 px = 192 ticks = 3.2 s | yes (the dark is the cue) | walk | gauge 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 5 | Coolant leak CLT-LEAK | coolant gauge, radiator glow | frost fan, DRIP, cavitation TICK, 384 px = 3.2 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s (single reservoir on the slice); gauge 8.0 s where a second exists | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 6 | Pump stall (STARVE) | pump sprite shown running | dark branch fixtures, no cavitation TICK, beam sweep, 240 px = 2.0 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 7 | Fuel flange drip | sight-glass | PUDDLE plus DRIP (glossy, flat, ember edge), 144 px = 72 ticks = 1.2 s | yes | walk | scanner 2.6 s (one tank sight-glass on the slice) | <= 120 s | VS-B |
| 8 | Hull fatigue toward breach | hull gauge | the groan TICK with `since_steps`, hairlines along ribs; listen for the next groan, up to one `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` period = 100 steps x `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 = 600 ticks = **10 s** (Graph-owned and cited, 20 s at the top of its range); 0 s of walk *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rung 1 for fault 8 was unvalued)* | no (hear) | none | another fixed hull gauge 8.0 s; scanner 2.6 s (`suit.integrity` measures the suit and does not count). **A comfort lie on this fault is stage 2 or deeper: reachable in play after the OQ-SN-38 ruling (SN-29)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | <= 120 s | VS-B |

Scan shortcut (every fault): lit channel, tool slot 3 `tool_scanner`, noise class TOOL (declared by Interaction, IA-30; OQ-SN-13 closed on this half), 2.6 s lower bound. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*

**Worst-case rung 1: a witness two hops away** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `T_room(2 hops) = ceil((d_room + 2 x D_HOP_ILLUS) / V_WALK) + 2 x T_CROSS_PROV` with `D_HOP_ILLUS` 480 px (10 tiles per compartment, illustrative, Layout's real figure replaces it) and `T_CROSS_PROV` 30 ticks; the single-instrument-loop caveat is that a carrying seat adds the drop to every scan figure.

| Fault | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rung 1, same compartment (D.7 table above) | 2.4 s | 1.6 s | 4.0 s | 3.2 s | 3.2 s | 2.0 s | 1.2 s | up to 10 s (listen) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Rung 1, two hops (worst case under `WITNESS_HOPS_MAX` 2) | 11.4 s | 10.6 s | 13.0 s | 12.2 s | 12.2 s | 11.0 s | 10.2 s | up to 10 s (listen; no walk) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Scan from where one stands (lower bound) | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s | 2.6 s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

All eight stay under the 15 s QA placeholder of AC-SN-134 (OQ-SN-18), and the table is why a two-hop witness is the point at which the scan wins (`d*(2)` = 2.5 tiles). **Per-class rows (deferred classes):** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

| Class | Rung 1 | Rung 2 | Rung 3 | Rung 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) phantom | look at the floor for footprints (M17) and wait for an Attention tell that does not come: 2 to 5 s, lit | walk through it (it neither pushes nor blocks): risk, 1 to 3 s | a saner crewmate, VS-B | `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s |
| (a) hidden real entity (HIDE) | its footprints and its own sounds remain: 0 extra, listen | a second view from another position | VS-B | `HIDE_LIFE_S` 8 to 20 s |
| (c) crewmates as horrors | the true voice and the true beam colour: 0 s | a second look from another position *(a crewmate has no instrument, OQ-SN-8)* | ask a saner crewmate | leave eligibility (`level` above `SAN_HARM_EXIT`): minutes, an escort home |
| (d) phantom salvage | carry it and listen for the weight breath-step (Interaction's VS-A `check_kind` HEFT, IA-30): pickup time only (drop stowed HEAVY first) | SCAN 2.6 s lower bound | PALETTE, VS-A | the trip home (the dearest) |

**Class (d) design test.** `C_check <= 0.05 x T_home` where `T_home` is the one-way trip home with the item, **from the pickup to the home install point** (the route to the pod console, the hop, and the home carry to the HARDPOINT or stow point; Boarding's reading (c), ASK-BS-N1), because the trip home is the dearest rung only when the item's worth is learned at the point of use. **Example:** `T_home` 120 s, SCAN 2.6 s: ratio 0.022, passes; `T_home` 40 s: 0.065 fails, which means an item that near is not worth a trip and the check is not the point. Boarding's finding C-BS-11 holds: a SCAN passes the test on no MVP route (0.076 for `htr_a` under this reading); it is not a defect at the MVP because class (d) ships none, and it binds VS-A junk placement (`T_home >= 20 x C_check`, Derelict Authoring's to place). `T_EQUIP` is 0 by Suit SE-11 (OQ-SN-6 closed). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*

#### D.8 `ambient_floor_invariant`

The `ambient_floor_invariant` formula is defined as:

`min over ring of AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring] >= SAN_TELL_OFF + AMBIENT_MARGIN` (a); `|f - t| >= STAGE_HYST` for every floor `f` and every stage threshold `t` in {`STAGE_ENTER[s]`, `STAGE_ENTER[s] + STAGE_HYST`} (b); `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring + 1] <= AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` (c); `max over ring of RING_EXPOSURE[ring] < REC_SETTLED` (d); `max over ring of RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE < DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` (e)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` | int mSU | 53000 / 52000 / 43000 | knob | level below which the ambient term stops; ring 1 lowered from 62000 by the user ruling on OQ-SN-38 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `AMBIENT_MARGIN` | int mSU | 5000 (safe 3000 to 8000: assertion (a) needs `min floor >= SAN_TELL_OFF + AMBIENT_MARGIN`, so the ceiling is 43000 - 35000 = 8000) | knob *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the range read 3000 to 10000 and violated this document's own assertion)* | clearance above `SAN_TELL_OFF` |
| `STAGE_HYST`, `STAGE_ENTER[s]` | int mSU | 2000; 70000 / 55000 / 25000 | knobs | stage geometry; stage 2 raised from 45000 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `RING_EXPOSURE`, `REC_SETTLED`, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`, `REC_IDLE` | int mSU/run | 0 / 4 / 8; 12; 20; 1 | knobs | the home-refuge and sub-cap relations |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per clause, evaluated at boot and on every retune; a missing floor for any ring refuses boot with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason, and no default of 0 exists. At the defaults: (a) 43000 >= 40000, margin 3000; (b) the nearest pairs are 53000 to 55000 (2000, exactly `STAGE_HYST`), 52000 to 55000 (3000), 53000 to 57000 (4000), 43000 to 55000 (12000), so it passes with the ring-1 floor sitting exactly on the clearance (it was the ring-3 floor against 45000 before the OQ-SN-38 ruling) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; (c) 53000 >= 52000 >= 43000; (d) 8 < 12; (e) 8 + 20 - 1 = 27 < 36. Calm waiting therefore cannot reach `tell_on`, stage 3 or `harm_eligible`. **Stage at each floor:** ring 1 stage 2 (2000 below the entry 55000 and 4000 below the exit 57000, so stage 2 holds); ring 2 stage 2 (3000 below the entry); ring 3 stage 2 (12000 below the entry, 16000 above the stage-3 exit 27000; the level sawtooths within 26 mSU below 43000); no floor is in stage 3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
**Example (time from `SAN_MAX`, net = `RING_EXPOSURE + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE`):** ring 1, net 19: stage 1 after `ceil(30000 / 19)` = 1579 runs = 315.8 s (5.3 min), stage 2 after `ceil(45001 / 19)` = 2369 runs = 473.8 s (7.9 min), floor 53000 after `ceil(47000 / 19)` = 2474 runs = 494.8 s (8.2 min). Ring 2, net 23: stage 1 after 1305 runs = 261.0 s, stage 2 after `ceil(45001 / 23)` = 1957 runs = 391.4 s, floor 52000 after 2087 runs = 417.4 s. Ring 3, net 27: stage 1 after 1112 runs = 222.4 s, stage 2 after `ceil(45001 / 27)` = 1667 runs = 333.4 s, floor 43000 after 2112 runs = 422.4 s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. At the floor the level sawtooths: one run above it loses the ring's aboard rate (19 / 23 / 27 for ring 1 / 2 / 3), then recovers 1 per run, so it stays within `[floor - 18, floor + 1]` in ring 1, `[floor - 22, floor + 1]` in ring 2 and `[floor - 26, floor + 1]` in ring 3. **Hangover:** a seat that left the ring-1 floor (53000, stage 2) and settles at home needs 334 runs = 66.8 s to cross the stage-2 exit at 57000 and `ceil(19000 / 12)` = 1584 runs = 316.8 s to cross the stage-1 exit at 72000 (was 166.8 s from the old 62000 floor); from the ring-3 floor, `ceil(14000 / 12)` = 1167 runs (233.4 s) clears stage 2 and 29000 / 12 = 2417 runs (483.4 s) clears stage 1 (both figures assume the home body is in ring 1, the MVP baseline WM-27; a home in ring 3 nets `REC_SETTLED - RING_EXPOSURE[3]` = 4 per run above its floor, so the same climb takes three times as long).

**Derivation of the stage-2 pair (user ruling on OQ-SN-38 option (b), 2026-10-27; D.4 and D.8 re-run in full)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Constraint 1, reachability.* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: the premise is restated; the ruled values stand. Web Map's `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` is a fuel-floor guarantee and not a visit length, and Web Map's D.10 visits run 14 to 18 minutes.)* The reachability relation is `SAN_MAX - L_ref_runs x net < STAGE_ENTER[2]`, where `net = RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE` and `L_ref_runs` is the reference visit length in runs, read from the registry (a typical-visit-length constant Web Map is asked to register, OQ-SN-44; until it exists AC-SN-171 uses the 14 minute low end of D.10, 4200 runs, and flags the read as provisional). A calm ring-1 sortie nets `RING_EXPOSURE[1] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE` = 0 + 20 - 1 = 19 mSU per run, so the relation holds for `L_ref_runs > 45000 / 19` = 2368.4 runs (473.7 s); at the 14 minute visit it holds for any `net >= 11`, that is `ABOARD_EXPOSURE >= 12` in ring 1, and at `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` 8 (the bottom of its safe range) it fails (net 7 needs 6429 runs = 21.4 min), so a retune below 12 must be re-checked against this relation. The earlier derivation used Web Map's `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks, 2400 runs, as the sortie: the level at the end of that guaranteed minimum is `100000 - 19 x 2400` = 54,400, which gives the lower bound `STAGE_ENTER[2] >= 54,401` for stage 2 to be reached even by the shortest guaranteed sortie; the ruled value is the round 55,000. Worked example: level 54,989 after `ceil(45001 / 19)` = 2369 runs = 473.8 s, 6.2 s inside the 480 s guaranteed-minimum sortie (and about 6 to 10 minutes before the end of a 14 to 18 minute visit). *Constraint 2, clearance (D.8 (b)).* `|AMBIENT_FLOOR[1] - t| >= STAGE_HYST` for every `t` in {55000, 57000, 70000, 72000, 25000, 27000}, so `AMBIENT_FLOOR[1] <= 55000 - 2000` = 53,000 (the other legal side, 59,000 and up, is excluded because the floor must sit below the entry for stage 2 to hold while the seat waits). 53,000 is the ruled value and sits exactly on the clearance; at 53,000 the exit 57000 is 4000 above the floor, so stage 2 holds. *Constraint 3, ordering (c).* 53000 >= 52000 >= 43000 with rings 2 and 3 unchanged. *Constraint 4, (a), (d), (e).* They read only the lowest floor (43000, unchanged), `RING_EXPOSURE`, `REC_SETTLED`, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` and the cap, so they are unchanged and pass. *Constraint 5, D.4 (i) to (v).* They read `STAGE_ENTER[1]`, `SAN_TELL_ON`, `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR)` and the cap, none of which moved: margins stay 2.4 / 23.4 / 5.6 / 53.4 / 5.6 s. *Stage geometry.* The entries are 70000 / 55000 / 25000, gaps 15,000 and 30,000 (each at least `2 x STAGE_HYST`); two thresholds still cannot be crossed inside one dwell (900 mSU against 15,000); the lowest floor 43000 is 16,000 above the stage-3 exit 27000, so calm waiting still cannot enter stage 3, set `tell_on` or reach `harm_eligible`. *What the pair costs, stated.* Stage 2 can now be reached by waiting alone in every ring; the ring-1 floor is first reached only after 2474 runs = 494.8 s, outside the 480 s guaranteed-minimum sortie, so a calm ring-1 sortie cut at that bound ends at 54,400 and not at the floor, while a full 14 to 18 minute visit ends at the floor (53000); the rest figures in SN-29 start from the floor as the worst case, which is therefore the normal end of a visit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*. *Rest budget the MVP assumes ("as computed by D.4").* The SN-29 table: from 53000, a ring-1 home reaches `SAN_MAX` in 3917 runs = 13.1 min, a ring-2 home in 19.6 min, a ring-3 home in 39.2 min.

#### D.9 `harm_hysteresis_margin`

The `harm_hysteresis_margin` formula is defined as:

`entry_edge_margin = SAN_HARM_EXIT - SAN_HARM_ENTER`; assertion `entry_edge_margin >= ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR) x max(DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN, REC_MAX)`; `exit_edge_window = ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR) x REC_MAX`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 204 (cited) | Interaction | length of a harm channel | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `SAN_HARM_ENTER`, `SAN_HARM_EXIT` | int mSU | 15000, 20000 | knobs | gate band |
| `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, `REC_MAX` | int mSU/run | 36, 15 | knobs | the fastest the level can move per run in each direction |

**Output Range:** `ceil(204 / 12)` = 17 runs per channel (HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS 204 = 17 runs of 12 ticks, from `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 300). The margin must be at least 17 x 36 = 612; the default 5000 passes at 8.2x (5000 / 612 = 8.17). The safe range for `SAN_HARM_EXIT` follows: `SAN_HARM_ENTER + 612` = 15612 and up (the formula's minimum) and, to keep an eligible actor from staying eligible for minutes, at most 25000. The **exit edge** is different: an actor who starts a channel while recovering within `exit_edge_window` = 17 x 15 = 255 mSU below `SAN_HARM_EXIT` can be pushed over it mid-channel, which is Interaction's `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` (AC-IA-88); the entry edge cannot flip from drift alone. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* **Recomputation (was 120 ticks = 10 runs):** runs = ceil(204 / 12) = 17 (was 10); entry margin required = 17 x max(36, 15) = 612 (was 360); ratio 5000 / 612 = 8.17x (was 13.9x); safe floor = 15000 + 612 = 15612 (was 15360); exit_edge_window = 17 x 15 = 255 (was 150); settled home recovery over a channel = 17 x 12 = 204 mSU.
**Example:** an eligible actor settling at home at `level` 19900 starts a channel: recovery 12 per run for 17 runs moves the level to 20104, above 20000, so the bit clears at the run that crosses (run 9, level 20008) and the channel aborts at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check. Starting at 19790 the level ends at 19994 and the channel completes (19800 would now end at 20004 and flip). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*

#### D.10 `horror_encounter_budget` *(E3; provisional on Horror)*

The `horror_encounter_budget` formula is defined as:

`pay_h = max(0, min(EXP_HORROR, HORROR_ENC_BUDGET - spent_h))` is horror `h`'s raw contribution to `E3` each run; `E3 = sum of pay_h over near h`; after the run's `net` is known, the **charge** is `delta = net(E3) - net(E3 = 0)` (both through the clamp of D.1; `delta >= 0`), spread over the near horrors in ascending ref order as `charge_h = min(pay_h, delta - sum of earlier charges)`, and `spent_h' = spent_h + charge_h` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: marginal charging, option (a) of OQ-SN-45; the budget was burned by contact the net cap absorbed)*; `spent_h' = 0` after `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S x 5` consecutive runs with `h` out of range; `min_encounters(a, b) = ceil((a - b) / HORROR_ENC_BUDGET)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
