|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EXP_HORROR` | int mSU/run | 20 (safe 0 to 28) | knob | rate while a registered HORROR is within `HORROR_NEAR_R` |
| `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` | int mSU | 3000 (safe 1500 to 6000) | knob | most one horror can charge in one encounter |
| `HORROR_ENC_RESET_S` | int s | 120 (safe 30 to 300) | knob | out-of-range time that ends an encounter |
| `HORROR_NEAR_R` | int px | 288 (safe 144 to 480) | knob | distance from the seat's foot origin to the horror's `pos_px`; no light condition |
| `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` | int | 4 | knob | tracked refs; a fifth evicts the stalest encounter |
| `spent_h` | int mSU | 0 to `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` | `sanity.ledger` `horror_enc[]` | spend in the current encounter |

**Output Range:** `pay_h` in 0 to 20; one horror charges at most 3000 mSU per encounter, reached after 150 runs = 30 s of contact when the net cap does not bind (when it binds, the charge is the marginal `delta`, so the encounter lasts longer and the budget is spent more slowly). **Farm bound** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: restated from the lowest ambient floor, where routine play starts, and over 1 to `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` concurrent horrors; the earlier figure of about 25 encounters and over an hour was measured from stage-1 entry (55000 mSU) with one horror only and is superseded)*: from `min(AMBIENT_FLOOR)` 43000 to `SAN_HARM_ENTER` 15000 is 28000 mSU. With `k` horrors in contact at once the contact phase nets `k x 3000 - runs_contact x REC_IDLE` and each 120 s gap repays `REC_IDLE x 600` = 600, and the contact phase ends when every `pay_h` is 0, so its length is found by running the marginal-charge split of this formula (ascending ref order, `charge_h = min(pay_h, delta - earlier charges)`), not by dividing: k = 1: 150 runs, a 750-run cycle losing 2250; k = 2: 173 runs (150 runs at `net` 36 splitting `delta` 37 as 20 and 17, then horror 2 alone for 23 runs), a 773-run cycle losing 5227; k = 3: 304 runs, a 904-run cycle losing 8096; k = 4: 343 runs, a 943-run cycle losing 11057. The **first run below 15000** from 43000 (the quantity AC-SN-25 asserts) is run 9053 for k = 1 (1810.6 s, 30.2 min), 3917 for k = 2 (783.4 s, 13.1 min), 2816 for k = 3 (563.2 s, 9.4 min) and 2050 for k = 4 (410 s, 6.8 min); these fall in cycle 13, 6, 4 and 3 respectively. (The end of the Nth encounter is a different, later point: 13 x 150 + 12 x 600 = 9150 runs = 1830 s for k = 1; it is not the crossing.) The minimum over k is therefore 410 s (k = 4), still above the cap-only floor of D.4 (v) (185.6 s), so the budget does not weaken the lead invariant, and it is an **upper bound on speed** (marginal charging only slows it). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4: contact lengths recomputed from the ascending-order marginal split (the earlier uniform 37-per-run split is superseded) and the times restated as first-crossing runs; the earlier end-of-encounter and uniform-split times are superseded)* Evict-and-retain (SN-30) means a fifth horror cannot refresh a spent budget, so these counts hold for any number of horrors in the world.
**Example:** a repelled horror held 250 px away: runs 1 to 150 pay 20 each (3000), run 151 onward pays `min(20, 0)` = 0; after the horror is out of range for 600 runs the budget resets to 3000 available.

#### D.11 `lie_rate_estimate` *(design-time calibration aid; not a second onset rule)*

The `lie_rate_estimate` formula is defined as:

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rewritten as a renewal model. The first model treated every gated event as an independent Poisson arrival with a fixed dead time `G`, ignoring that `LIES_MAX` 1 together with the PENDING span blocks the next onset at stage 1 and that at most one onset lands per entry batch, so it overstated the stage-1 lie rate and the 65 / 222 / 400 alternative inherited the error.)* `P_batch = 1 - (1 - p)^N`; `D(s) = E[max(G, d + L)]` at stage 1 (the PENDING plus ACTIVE span blocks the next onset, `LIES_MAX` 1) and `D(s) = G` at stages 2 and 3 while `N_avg <= LIES_MAX - 1`; `T_on = D(s) + 1 / (rho x P_batch)`; `n10 = 600 / T_on`; `N_avg = min(LIES_MAX[s], L_avg / T_on)`; inverse `P_batch = 1 / (rho x (T_target - D(s)))` and `ONSET_PM = round(1000 x (1 - (1 - P_batch)^(1 / N)))`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rho` | float per s | 0.025 reference (one compartment entry per 40 s; safe 0.01 to 0.075) | reference; replaced by the Debug Overlay's measured entries per minute | entry-batch arrival rate for one seat *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `N` | int | 4 reference (surfaces lit per entry; safe 1 to 8) | reference, the Derelict Authoring floor of 3 or more (OQ-SN-11); replaced by the measured count | gated events per batch; `lambda_e = rho x N` = 0.1 per s is the event rate the first model used and the Overlay's `eligible_entries_per_min` still measures *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `P_batch` | float | 0 to 1 | derived | probability that a batch yields an onset: at most one onset lands per batch because `LIE_GAP_S` is at least 5 s and a batch is processed inside one run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `D(s)` | float s | stage 1 about 83.5; stage 2 = 25; stage 3 = 10 | derived | mean dead time after an onset; at stage 1 the invisible delay `d` (2 to 5 s) plus the ACTIVE life `L` (40 to 120 s) exceeds the 45 s gap in all but 2.1 percent of draws (136 of the 6416 equally likely (life, delay) pairs of 200 to 600 runs and 10 to 25 runs have `d + L <= 225` runs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: the earlier figure was 3.6 %, which a recount of the discrete draws does not support; `D(s)` 83.5 is unchanged to within 0.1 s)* |
| `p` | float | `ONSET_PM[s] / 1000` | knob | accept probability |
| `G` | int s | `LIE_GAP_S[s]` 45 / 25 / 10 | knob | dead time after an onset |
| `L_avg` | float s | 80 | mean of the 40 to 120 life band | mean lie life |
| `T_target` | float s | 250 / 120 / 80 for stages 1 to 3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-derived; was 200 / 70 / 35)* | playtest alternative; domain `T_target > D(s) + 1 / rho` (123.5 / 65 / 50 s), else the inverse returns more than 1000 and is clamped to 1000 | target mean time between onsets |

**Output Range:** `T_on` is at least `D(s) + 1 / rho` (`P_batch` cannot exceed 1: even a sure accept waits for the next entry batch); `n10` the onsets per 10 minutes; `N_avg` the mean number of ACTIVE lies by Little's law, clamped to `LIES_MAX[s]` and valid while it stays below `LIES_MAX - 1` (above that the cap blocks onsets and the model overstates `T_on` falling; **the condition is marginally violated at stage 2 on the reference rate, `N_avg` 1.03 against a limit of 1, so 77.6 s is a slight underestimate and not exact**). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the numbers are re-derived.)* At the default `ONSET_PM` with `rho` 0.025 and `N` 4: stage 1 `P_batch` 0.478, `T_on` = 83.5 + 83.7 = **167.2 s**, `n10` 3.6, `N_avg` **0.48**; stage 2 `P_batch` 0.760, `T_on` = 25 + 52.6 = 77.6 s, `n10` 7.7, `N_avg` 1.03; stage 3 `P_batch` 0.959, `T_on` = 10 + 41.7 = 51.7 s, `n10` 11.6, `N_avg` 1.55 (cap 4). The first model's figures (111.7 / 58.3 / 28.2 s, `N_avg` 0.72 / 1.37 / 2.84) are superseded. The inverse gives the playtest alternative for a gentler curve: `ONSET_PM` **66 / 128 / 191** for `T_target` 250 / 120 / 80 s (stage 1: `P_batch` = 1 / (0.025 x (250 - 83.5)) = 0.240, `p` = 1 - 0.760^0.25 = 0.066; stage 2: 1 / (0.025 x 95) = 0.421, `p` = 0.128; stage 3: 1 / (0.025 x 70) = 0.571, `p` = 0.191). *(Orchestrator ruling kept: the defaults stay the rulings' 150 / 300 / 550 and this formula is a calibration aid, the decision rests with the first playtest. The stage-1 default now gives a lie ACTIVE about **48 percent** of the time on the reference rate (the design review's independent estimate was about 53 percent; the difference is the treatment of the invisible PENDING span and of the dead time, the two agree that the earlier 72 percent was too high). Two consequences: the stage-1 default is closer to "subtle, gauge-only" than the first model said; and the live-lie count at stage 3 is bounded by the compartment-entry rate, not by `LIES_MAX` 4, because one onset lands per entry batch, so an INTENSE stage 3 is raised by `N`, `rho` and `ONSET_PM`, which was a user question, OQ-SN-30. Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): keep `ONSET_PM` 150 / 300 / 550 and let the Debug Overlay's measured `N` and `rho` decide any later raise. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The result depends on `rho` and `N` more than on `ONSET_PM`, so the measured counters replace the reference.)*
**Example:** measured `rho` 0.0125 per s (one entry per 80 s) with `N` 4 at stage 2 with `ONSET_PM` 300: `P_batch` = 1 - 0.7^4 = 0.760, `T_on` = 25 + 1 / (0.0125 x 0.760) = 25 + 105.3 = 130.3 s, `n10` 4.6, `N_avg` 0.61 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: re-derived with the renewal model; the first model gave 91.7 s, 6.5 and 0.87 for `lambda_e` 0.05)*. **Domain checks and clamps:** `p` in [0, 1] (`ONSET_PM` 0 to 1000), `N >= 1`, `rho > 0`; `P_batch` is clamped to 1; the inverse requires `T_target > D(s) + 1 / rho` and otherwise returns `ONSET_PM` 1000 with a warning that the target is unreachable at that entry rate.

#### D.12 `immunity_sweep_ratio` *(design-time; the check on `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S`)*

The `immunity_sweep_ratio` formula is defined as:

`SD = N_s x (t_check + t_move) / min(DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S, t_dwell)`; a sweep is dominant when `SD < 0.25` at stage 2 or deeper

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `N_s` | int | 1+ | Derelict Authoring / slice content | lie-eligible surfaces in the compartment |
| `t_check` | float s | 2 | `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 / `TICK_RATE` | one scan |
| `t_move` | float s | 1.5 | design assumption (reach is 0.6 s of walk; 1.5 s to reposition) | between scans |
| `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` | int s | 60 (safe 45 to 120) | knob | immunity window; assertion `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S >= max(LIE_GAP_S)` (60 >= 45) |
| `t_dwell` | float s | 1+ | the player's plan | how long the seat intends to stay |

**Output Range:** `SD` above 0; below 0.25 the sweep costs less than a quarter of the stay and "scan everything on entry" is a free habit. **Example at `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60:** `N_s` 4, dwell 40 s: `4 x 3.5 / 40` = 0.35 (not dominant); `N_s` 2, dwell 40: 0.175 (dominant, hence the advisory 3-surface authoring floor, OQ-SN-11); `N_s` 4, dwell 120: `14 / 60` = 0.233 (marginally dominant, a legitimate purchase for a long task). At 120 s the third case would be 0.117, which is why 60 is the default. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **targeted scanning.** Every lie carries a gleam and a motion bit, so after the first lie the player scans the surfaces that visibly glide, not the room; the sweep ratio above is the worst-case habit, and the cost of the expected habit is `TS = N_g x (t_check + t_move) / min(L_avg, t_dwell)` with `N_g` the number of gliding surfaces in view (at most `LIES_MAX[s]`), plus a noise term `N_g x FIELD_CHECK_TICKS x w_tool` in Attention units, where `w_tool` is the AU weight Attention and Interaction assign to the TOOL class per tick (**[UNV]**: not valued in any GDD or in the registry, `NOISE_PER_TICK` lists walk / run / carry only). At the reference (`N_g` 1, `t_check` 2 s, `t_move` 1.5 s, `L_avg` 80 s, `t_dwell` 40 s) `TS` = 3.5 / 40 = 0.0875: targeted scanning is cheap in time by design, and its second currency is the noise term. The full cost model for a "rebuilt" D.12, including the noise weight and the scanner's role once detection is free, is left to the playtest, the default applied for OQ-SN-39 on 2026-10-27 (confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)) being no scanner additions at the MVP *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The rung-mix telemetry AC-SN-165 measures which rung players actually use.)*

#### D.13 `phantom_response_bound` *(class a; design-time; [UNV] Attention units)*

The `phantom_response_bound` formula is defined as:

`extra_noise = response_ticks x (NOISE_PER_TICK_run - NOISE_PER_TICK_walk)`; design bound `extra_noise <= PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM x ATT_THRESHOLD / 1000` with `PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM` 250

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `response_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` x `TICK_RATE` | the player | ticks spent running from a phantom |
| `NOISE_PER_TICK` run, walk | int AU | 4, 1 (cited) | Attention registry | noise per tick |
| `ATT_THRESHOLD` | int AU | 6000 (cited) | Attention registry | the breach threshold |
| `PHANTOM_RESPONSE_MAX_PM` | int permille | 250 | design bound, not a knob | share of the threshold one encounter may cost |

**Output Range:** raw noise units, before Attention's decay `TAU_ATT_TICKS` (not re-derived here, **[UNV]**). **Example:** a typical 6 s sprint: `360 x 3` = 1080 AU <= `250 x 6000 / 1000` = 1500, passes. A sprint for a phantom's full 20 s life: `1200 x 3` = 3600 AU, 60 percent of the threshold before decay, which fails the bound; the escape is Attention's decay and `PHANTOM_GAP_S` 60 s, and the question is carried in Open Questions (the stage-3 `PHANTOM_LIFE_S` upper end may need to drop to 12 s: `720 x 3` = 2160, still over; the bound is a playtest target, not a proof).

## Edge Cases

Format: **If [condition]**: [exact outcome]. [rationale]. Each bullet ends with its id `EC-SN-n`, which Acceptance Criteria cite. Every outcome below is a defined result of the rules above, never "handle gracefully".

#### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If `level` is exactly on a stage threshold (70000, 55000 or 25000 on entry; 72000, 57000 or 27000 on exit)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the current stage holds, because entry is strictly below and exit strictly above (D.2). Equality must not flip a stage, or a rate that lands on a threshold would chatter. *(EC-SN-1)*
- **If `level` reaches 0**: it clamps at 0 (D.1 `clamp(level - net, 0, SAN_MAX)`); the stage stays 3, `tell_on` stays set and `harm_eligible` is set once the dwell has passed; exposure keeps being computed but cannot lower it further, and recovery lifts it as usual. Nothing happens at zero: there is no breakdown, no death, no new state (Pillar 1: the world, not a threshold, is the consequence). *(EC-SN-2)*
- **If `level` is `SAN_MAX` and `net` is negative (a settled home at full sanity)**: it clamps at `SAN_MAX`; the recovery is wasted. At stage 0 the onset path is not entered, which costs nothing (the pre-draw gate "stage at least 1" fails before any draw). *(EC-SN-3)*
- **If a second or third startle arrives while `pending` is full**: `pending'` is `min(PENDING_MAX, pending - draw + startle_add)`, so 1500 + 1500 = 3000 fits exactly and a third 1500 is discarded entirely. Overflow only ever reduces drift, the safe direction; the debt is bounded at two stacked startles, about 177 runs (35.4 s) of extra descent after a BREACH, aboard in ring 1 at CALM above the floor (headroom 36 - 19 = 17; 3000 / 17 rounds up to 177). *(EC-SN-4)*
- **If a restore finds a stored value out of range (a `level` above `SAN_MAX`, a negative counter, a `stage` of 4, an unknown lie `kind`, a ledger `end_run` before `start_run`)**: the restore of that block fails with a closed `SANITY_RESTORE_*` reason; the value is never clamped (SL-15). A clamped value would let a corrupt save quietly change the player's sanity and would break `state_hash` equality (G4). *(EC-SN-5)*
- **If `ring_of(actor_section(actor))` is unknown or outside 1 to 3 (a section Web Map has not resolved, or a future fourth ring)**: the seat uses ring 1 and Sanity logs it once per seat (the AT-6 fallback). That is the shallowest, least-draining reading; the real depth data is Web Map's (WM-27 publishes it; baseline ring 1) *(Web Map & Transit, Designed; status refreshed 2026-10-27 — design review)*, and the boot lint refuses a ring with no `AMBIENT_FLOOR`. *(EC-SN-6)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If `AMBIENT_FLOOR` is missing for any ring in use**: boot refuses with a closed `SANITY_BOOT_*` reason. A silent default floor of 0 would remove the ambient bound and let calm waiting reach `SAN_HARM_ENTER` (SN-29). *(EC-SN-7)*
- **If `E3` is computed with five horrors near**: only `HORROR_TRACK_MAX` 4 encounters are tracked; the stalest is evicted and the fifth is paid from a fresh budget. `E` can then reach 100 or more, but `net` is clamped to `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 36, so the lead invariants hold. The count is a design absurdity, not a hazard. *(EC-SN-8)*
- **If a tuning change makes `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN` 40 with nothing else changed**: D.4 (i) and (iii) fail ((ii) passes at 75 s) and boot refuses (`SANITY_BOOT_LEAD_INVARIANT`). The lead invariants are build assertions, not guidance. *(EC-SN-9)*

#### Simultaneous rules and ordering inside one run

- **If two stage thresholds would both be crossed in one run**: impossible under the cap (`25 x 36` = 900 mSU per dwell against gaps of at least 15000 between entry thresholds, *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: was 20000*). It can occur only through a restore or a debug force, where the stored stage is kept after a restore and a forced recompute moves directly to the target with the dwell saturated (SN-4). One transition per run, the deeper applying if two dwell timers expire together. *(EC-SN-10)*
- **If the startle is credited on the first CALM run after a breach and the cap binds**: the credit run's `E2` is 0 (CALM), the 1500 goes into `pending` and drains only from later headroom (D.1 example E: 32 runs settled at home, 89 runs on a ring-1 derelict). Startle never bypasses the cap, so the lead invariants stay true build assertions. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: the credit is at SUBSIDING end, the only point at which Attention publishes BREACHED; OQ-SN-42 closed.)* *(EC-SN-11)*
- **If a `field_check` completes on the same hook tick as the lie's `end_run`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the check lands first because `interaction.step` precedes `sanity.drift` (SN-2): the lie is DEBUNKED with immunity stamped, and the hook then finds nothing to expire. The first draft said expiry is evaluated first; with the completion call in a hook ordered before Sanity's, that no longer holds, and the difference is not observable to the player: both end with no mark, the same glide-return and the same uniform immunity. *(EC-SN-12)*
- **If a `field_check` completes in the same tick that the lie's witness ends** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a repair is not a falsification call, SN-17)*: Interaction's `interaction.step` calls `record_falsification` on the completion tick and runs before the `sanity.drift` hook in that tick (the `after` edge of SN-2), so the lie is already DEBUNKED and the immunity is stamped before the hook would have INVALIDATED it. Immunity on a subject whose witness has gone is harmless and uniform. *(EC-SN-13)*
- **If two seats gain a lit-entry event on the same run**: seats are processed in ascending PlayerId, each with its own streams and counters, so neither seat's draws depend on the other's; only the order of work differs. *(EC-SN-14)*
- **If a stage change and a lie activation land on the same run**: stage first (SN-2 order), so the activation re-check and the cap use the new stage's `LIES_MAX`; an existing lie is never ended by the change (SN-16). *(EC-SN-15)*
- **If the seat changes compartment while lit-entry events are held**: the held events are discarded exactly as SN-16 states: `evented[]` empty, no stamp, no draw, no counter advance, `batch_end_run` cleared, `entry_pending` set; a change on `batch_end_run` itself still discards (the change check precedes the batch close); a change one run later finds them processed. *(EC-SN-64)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*
- **If a real BREACH or a real TELL begins while a comfort-direction lie (a FALSE_OK, or a GLIDE or swap toward health, L-D) is live on the same loop** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the real Attention outputs are untouched (AT-17). If the loop's L-G guard fails (the seat's compartment band for the O2, COOLANT, POWER and FUEL loops, the hull-plate margin for INTEGRITY, SN-15) the lie is INVALIDATED at the next run (L-G); if the fault is elsewhere the lie continues and the seat has the tell, the Suit cues and the witness to read. A lie never delays, replaces or fakes the tell. *(EC-SN-16)*
- **If a harm channel's gate flips mid-channel**: Interaction ABORTs with `NOT_ELIGIBLE` at the next `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` check (AC-IA-88). It can arise from drift only at the exit edge and only within `exit_edge_window` 255 mSU of `SAN_HARM_EXIT` while the actor recovers (D.9); QA reaches it by settling an eligible actor at home. The entry edge cannot flip from drift. *(EC-SN-17)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*

#### Degenerate strategies

- **If the player stands in a calm derelict to lower their own sanity (a self-harm lever)**: the ambient term stops at `AMBIENT_FLOOR[ring]` (53000 / 52000 / 43000), all above `SAN_TELL_OFF` + `AMBIENT_MARGIN` (D.8 (a)), so waiting can reach stage 2 in every ring (ring 1 after 473.8 s, ring 2 after 391.4 s, ring 3 after 333.4 s; the ruled consequence of OQ-SN-38) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* and can never set `tell_on`, enter stage 3 or reach `harm_eligible`. Only Attention events and real horror contact lower it further, and those are the world's. *(EC-SN-18)*
- **If the player holds a repelled horror at the edge of `HORROR_NEAR_R` to farm exposure**: the encounter budget caps one horror at `HORROR_ENC_BUDGET` 3000 mSU per encounter (30 s of contact), and the encounter resets only after 120 s out of range, during which `REC_IDLE` repays 600 (D.10). The farm is bounded from the lowest ambient floor: 13 encounters and about 30 minutes for one horror, down to about 7 minutes with four concurrent horrors (D.10), and the cap makes it no faster than any other descent. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED: was "more than an hour for about 25 encounters", measured from stage-1 entry with one horror; also, the budget is charged with the marginal contribution and survives eviction.)* *(EC-SN-19)*
- **If the player scans every eligible surface on entering a compartment**: each scan costs `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 (lit, reach, Interaction's noise class) and immunity lasts `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` 60 s uniformly, so a sweep of 4 surfaces for a 40 s stay has `SD` 0.35 and is not dominant (D.12). With 2 or fewer eligible surfaces it is dominant; that is the advisory authoring floor (OQ-SN-11), not a Sanity mutator. *(EC-SN-20)*
- **If the player turns the lamp off and on, or sweeps the beam, to spam lit-entry events**: a change in `lit` caused only by the seat's own lamp inside the same compartment is not an event, and each subject has `LIT_ENTRY_DEBOUNCE_S` 20 s. Re-entering the compartment re-sets `entry_pending` and re-fires the batch, but `LIE_GAP_S`, `LIES_MAX` and the debounce gate it, and a gated-out event draws nothing. *(EC-SN-21)*
- **If the player stays unlit for longer than any timer and then lights up in a compartment with live gauges**: `entry_pending` has persisted since entry, so the batch window opens on the first run `C(P)` is non-empty and each first-lit subject forms its one visit event (SN-16). There is no free dodge by waiting in the dark. Staying unlit gives zero onsets, and gives up the lit-only verbs and the readings (SN-16); it carries no sanity cost (SN-6). *(EC-SN-22)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the player quits and reloads, or dies and reloads, to reroll a lie**: the ledger, the per-subject visit counters, `evented[]`, `batch_end_run` and `entry_pending` restore exactly, so the same actions replay the same draws (SN-24). No `restore_reason` changes content. The residual is that identical actions after a death draw the same per-subject dice sequence, though not at the same moment (the Attention draws around a restored seat re-key per restore epoch, X8), priced by the death. *(EC-SN-23)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, RECOMMENDED)*
- **If the player burns draws in one room to change what happens in another**: it cannot; the lie stream is keyed `"sanity.lie/<PlayerId>/<stable_id>"` per subject, so only gated events on that subject advance its counter (CQ2). The only way to change a subject's dice is to visit the subject. *(EC-SN-24)*
- **If the player repairs a subject repeatedly to farm the "came undone" weight**: at the MVP default `REPAIR_BIAS_W` 1 there is no weight to farm (user ruling on OQ-SN-40: the 2x repair bias is dropped from the MVP), so a repair neither raises nor lowers `ONSET_PM`, and a lie on a just-repaired subject occurs at the base rate only, each onset still passing `LIE_GAP_S`, `LIES_MAX`, the severity and witness rules, and L-I immunity after any check. The `repaired_recent[]` field is kept, reserved and always empty at the MVP (no writes), so the knob can be re-enabled with no format change *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2: the stamp is written post-MVP from a Graph repair event, SN-17)*; if it is re-enabled (`REPAIR_BIAS_W` above 1) the weight multiplies `ONSET_PM` for `REPAIR_BIAS_S` 60 s and the loop yields at most one extra lie per gap. A lie on a subject the player just repaired is still possible at the base rate and remains the intended shape of the fiction. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the review's overlap premise with `DEBUNK_IMMUNE_S` does not hold, because Interaction does not adopt the repair-completion debunk (IA-30, OQ 32) and a repair stamps no immunity; the creative disagreement between the level-designer and the game-designer was resolved by the user, OQ-SN-40.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(EC-SN-25)*
- **If an actor causes harm hoping to be rewarded or to lower a witness's sanity**: `record_harm_event` changes nothing but the `incidents` ring (no level change, no reward, no recovery, no E4 in the MVP; SN-13). Harm is a story the web told, not a lever. *(EC-SN-26)*

#### Save, restore and replay

- **If a restore lands mid-dwell (`harm_below_runs` or `stage_dwell` partly counted)**: the persisted counters resume and the bit does not reset; the restore never recomputes the stage (SN-4, SN-11). *(EC-SN-27)*
- **If a snapshot is taken between hook runs**: `evented[]`, `batch_end_run`, `entry_pending`, `last_cid`, `last_onset_run`, the debounce stamps and the visit counters are persisted ledger state and are not rebuilt from the world (every stamp is an absolute `run = T div 12`, SN-1 units), so an uninterrupted run and a restored run hold the same set at their next run and `state_hash` agrees (G4; SN-1). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `lit_prev` became `evented[]`.)* *(EC-SN-28)*
- **If a restore lands while a lie is PENDING**: the entry keeps its `start_run`, `end_run` and activation delay and activates on schedule after its SN-15 re-check; nothing is re-drawn (no counter moves in `post_restore`). *(EC-SN-29)*
- **If the ledger holds a well-formed `subject_ref` that no longer resolves after the Graph and Layout blocks have restored** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING)*: the restore succeeds and changes nothing persisted; `sanity.perception` is built from the ledger as the uninterrupted run holds it; and the next hook run INVALIDATES the entry with no immunity and prunes its `visit`, `evented[]`, `debounce[]`, `immune[]` and `repaired_recent[]` entries, which is exactly what the uninterrupted run does. The first draft treated this as corruption on the premise that destruction is always pruned "in the same run", which is false: Sanity runs only when `T mod 12 = 0` while Save/Load snapshots at any tick boundary (SL:453), so a subject destroyed or salvaged between runs and followed by an autosave is a valid save, and the MVP death rule (restart from the last autosave) would have hard-failed on it. A **malformed** ref (wrong shape, out-of-range ids) is still `SANITY_RESTORE_SHAPE` or `_COUNTER_RANGE`, never clamped. `state_hash` parity with the uninterrupted run holds at every tick, because nothing is pruned at restore (AC-SN-159). *(EC-SN-30)*
- **If a death stub reloads the last autosave at stage 3**: the level, stage, ledger and counters come back from the autosave (at most `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` of progress lost); death is not a cure (SL H3), and a seat already low wakes still low. *(EC-SN-31)*
- **If a save carries a `last_tell_epoch` equal to the live `tell_epoch` and `tell_outcome` BREACHED**: no startle is added on the first run after load; the epoch was already credited (SN-7). A restore never double-credits and never drops the pending debt. *(EC-SN-32)*
- **If a save has a `schema_version` the loader does not support**: the block refuses with the SL-15 closed reason; Sanity ships `supported_versions` [1] and no migration, so a newer file is refused rather than guessed. *(EC-SN-33)*
- **If `post_restore` runs**: `sanity.perception` is rebuilt in full from `sanity.state` and `sanity.ledger`, so the first RUNNING frame already carries the right lies (SN-23); a one-frame unfiltered view would be a free oracle. *(EC-SN-34)*

#### Hallucination interplay

- **If the seat leaves the compartment mid-lie**: the lie's clock keeps running in sim time; the entry stays listed (`away` true) and the Filter draws it only if the surface is visible; there is no return animation on leaving or on coming back (SN-16, SN-23); leaving for `LIE_LIFE_S` clears it, which is the time rung with a trip attached. *(EC-SN-35)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, G6 GAP, supersedes the 2026-10-27 delegated G1 listing rule)*
- **If the lying subject is destroyed or salvaged mid-lie**: the entry is INVALIDATED at the next run, with no immunity (a destroyed subject cannot be immune) and no EXPIRED stamp; `visit[p][u]` is pruned. *(EC-SN-36)*
- **If the witness ends mid-lie (the fault was repaired)**: a comfort-direction lie (FALSE_OK or equivalent, L-D) is INVALIDATED at the next run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, because L-W no longer holds; a FALSE_FAIL lie ends when the claimed failure is now true. In both cases the true state equals the displayed state or the witness is gone. *(EC-SN-37)*
- **If the true state becomes equal to the displayed state mid-lie (a real fault arises on a subject shown broken)**: INVALIDATED silently; a lie that says what is true is not a lie and leaves no leak to explain. *(EC-SN-38)*
- **If `field_check` targets a subject with no lie**: `record_falsification` stamps the uniform immunity, nothing visible changes and the level is unchanged (SN-17); a clean check leaks nothing. *(EC-SN-39)*
- **If two seats look at the same gauge at different stages**: each seat has an independent ledger entry; seat A may see a lie where seat B sees the truth, with no shared state. This is what makes the saner-crewmate rung work (VS-B). *(EC-SN-40)*
- **If a subject is eligible for both FALSE_OK and FALSE_FAIL**: the kind comes from the subject's true state (degraded or failed gives FALSE_OK; healthy gives FALSE_FAIL), then GLIDE and SPRITE_SWAP are drawn by `KIND_W` where reach allows; each candidate kind's **direction** (L-D) is fixed by the same true state, and the weighted pick sums only the legal-kind set, so a lie never carries a direction its rails did not check. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(EC-SN-41)*
- **If every instrument on a loop would be lying at once**: the lie is ineligible (L-2I); in the MVP the scanner is honest, so a single-instrument loop (the fuel sight-glass, the reservoir) is lie-eligible only because the scanner counts, and only for a seat that is not carrying a module (Suit SE-11: `equip_tool` is `BUSY` while carrying; no draw is made for a carrying seat, SN-15 L-2I). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(EC-SN-42)*
- **If the stage falls while more lies are live than the new stage's `LIES_MAX` allows**: no lie is removed; no new onset occurs until the live count is below the new cap, and the excess runs to expiry (a vanishing lie at a boundary would be a tell of the boundary). *(EC-SN-43)*
- **If a surface is itself the witness of another lie**: it is never a lying surface (a witness is a consequence-channel item); the candidate is skipped without a draw. *(EC-SN-44)*
- **If the first lie of a world has no fault-free lit subject to land on**: the onset is deferred (SN-16): no lie of any kind is raised, **no draw is made and no counter advances** (the first-lie restriction is part of the legal-kind set, so `sum_W` is 0), and `lies_seen` stays 0 until a fault-free lit subject exists (the home ship is the expected place). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(EC-SN-45)*
- **If class (b) is switched off at runtime (`CLASSES_ENABLED`)**: live lies expire normally and no new onsets occur; no snapshot format changes. *(EC-SN-46)*
- **If a phantom spawns while Attention is CALM (class a, deferred)**: `attention.view`, `TellAudit` and `emitters()` are unchanged (AC-AT-73); a phantom never creates a tell or a calm. *(EC-SN-47)*
- **If a hidden real entity (HIDE) enters `HIDE_SAFE_R`, its pre-act sub-tell, BREACH or SUBSIDING-in-locus**: the HIDE collapses that run and the next frame draws the body; its footprints were never hidden. *(EC-SN-48)*

#### Seat lifecycle and co-op hooks

- **If a seat is DOWNED**: it keeps drifting and keeps onset (exposure is real while down); a DEAD seat freezes, and in the MVP the death stub replaces the frozen state with the restored one (SN-3). *(EC-SN-49)*
- **If a seat unbinds and rebinds (VS-B)**: the stored state under its PlayerId is kept, with no drift while unbound; there is no join-to-reset lever. A PlayerId with no stored state starts at `SAN_MAX`. *(EC-SN-50)*
- **If two seats are both `harm_eligible` (VS-B)**: each is drawn as a horror to the other, each has its own victim tell, and either may start a lit channel; the first to complete kills and `record_harm_event` logs it. The gate never reads the target's level, only the attacker's bit. *(EC-SN-51)*
- **If an eligible actor has no valid target (the single-seat MVP)**: the bit is computed and queryable but nothing happens; class (c) rendering is disabled by `CLASSES_ENABLED` and exercised with stub actors. *(EC-SN-52)*
- **If a damaged home body has no compartment with all bands OK**: `settled` is false everywhere, recovery is `REC_IDLE` 1, and the level does not recover: a ring-1 home has E1 0, so the level still climbs by 1 per run toward `SAN_MAX`; a ring-2 home falls at +3 per run to the floor and sawtooths within `[52000 - 2, 52001]`; a ring-3 home falls at +7 and sawtooths within `[43000 - 6, 43001]`, so the level hovers at its ring's floor only in rings 2 and 3; the cure is repairing one compartment to all-OK bands (SN-8). The worst ambient state of a damaged home is its ring's floor, never lower without Attention or horror events. *(EC-SN-53)*
- **If `REC_HOME_BASE` is proposed again**: it is not adopted (CQ3); a playtest signal that a damaged home is a sanity trap is the reopen condition. *(EC-SN-54)*
- **If debug `force_level` crosses a threshold**: the stage and bit update through the same D.2 and D.3 path with the dwell saturated, the ledger is untouched, and the cap invariants are bypassed (SN-9 debug exception); this is never a player surface. *(EC-SN-55)*
- **If a fixture relights, or a glowstick comes to rest, in the seat's compartment after a subject has had its visit event** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: replaces the `lit_static` clause. `lit_static` is published, EM-25, and the rule no longer needs it)*: nothing happens; the subject is in `evented[]` for this visit and has no second event (SN-16). A subject that was dark at entry and is first lit later in the visit gets its one event at that moment, whatever lit it. *(EC-SN-56)*
- **If no node-to-subject mapping exists for a gauge (nobody registered it as an OBJECT subject)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the mapping is now authored data with an ERROR lint, `INSTRUMENT_NO_BINDING`, IW-31, DA-34; this case is a content defect, not a design gap)*: `lit(ref)` answers like unlit (EM-21), so that gauge is never a candidate; the lie set shrinks, and the lint catches it at authoring (AC-SN-134, AC-SN-161). *(EC-SN-57)*
- **If the seat is IN_POD mid-leg**: E1 = `RING_EXPOSURE[held_ring]` with no aboard term, recovery is `REC_IDLE`, no lit-entry event fires in the pod (the pod has no lie-eligible module-body subject), and live lies keep their sim-time clocks (SN-16). *(EC-SN-58)*
- **If a hidden horror gouges a duct or cable (a WORN descriptor, not a trail)**: that descriptor is excluded from the candidate set and any live lie on it is INVALIDATED at the next run; the gouge reads true to every seat (SN-15 L-C). *(EC-SN-59)*
- **If a live lie's derived direction no longer equals its stored direction** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: for example a COMFORT lie on a degraded subject whose true state rises to healthy (the display now equals the truth), or an ALARM lie on a healthy subject that genuinely degrades to the displayed band: the lie is INVALIDATED silently at the next run, with no immunity, and leaves by the same glide-return as any other end. A direction flip across the truth (a comfort lie that would now read as an alarm lie) is the same case; a lie is never re-labelled. *(EC-SN-60)*
- **If the legal-kind set is empty at an event** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: the event is refused at the pre-draw gate with no `rng_int` call and no counter advance, `visit[p][u]` unchanged, the debounce stamp written, the subject recorded in `evented[]` for this visit. Cases: a FAILED subject at stage 1, a DEGRADED lethal-mode subject at stage 1, a machine-sprite surface with no frame pair, a single-instrument-loop gauge for a carrying seat, a first-lie event with no fault-free lit subject (SN-16, D.5). *(EC-SN-61)*
- **If a seat picks up a module after a comfort lie on a single-instrument loop has become ACTIVE** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: the lie is not re-evaluated against carrying (SN-15 L-2I, the no-uncaused-vanish reason); the seat can drop the module and scan, a priced act. A lie still PENDING at that moment is INVALIDATED at its activation re-check. *(EC-SN-62)*
- **If a glowstick is thrown into an adjacent compartment** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; new)*: `C(P)` is read for the seat's own compartment only, so subjects it lights in the other compartment produce no event for the seat; spill that lights a subject inside the seat's own compartment gives that subject its single visit event like any first illumination. Zero draws for the first case. *(EC-SN-63)*

## Dependencies

Status vocabulary: **designed** (GDD on disk, header "Designed"); **in flight** (GDD on disk as a draft, `Status: In Design`); **undesigned-provisional** (no file; this GDD is the first writer of its half). Type is the strength of the dependency for this system; "Counterpart row" says whether the neighbour's own GDD already lists Sanity (design-docs rule: dependencies must be bidirectional).

**Soft cycle Sanity to Perception Filter (IDX:267).** "Sanity decides what is perceived; the Filter draws it. Resolution: Sanity is authoritative and writes only perception state; the Filter reads that state and writes only CanvasItem/Material/Light2D properties ... The Filter never calls back." Adopted: no Sanity symbol reads a Filter value, and the Filter holds no handle on `sanity.state` or `sanity.ledger`. The Filter's own write set is narrower than the index quote: `CanvasItem` and material properties only, with decoys drawn by Lighting (LO-21).

**Index corrections owed (routed, not edited here).** The Graph to Sanity edge is missing (soft, LSG:867); Interaction to Sanity is Hard and missing in the index's Interaction row (IA:824); the Sanity to Save/Load edge is a design-time contract and not a runtime call (SL OQ-15); the Attention row in the index should read `phase`, `tier`, `tell_outcome` and `tell_epoch`, not `level_pm` (C11); the index description of the kernel row ("commands") is refreshed in SN-17 (C13).

**Cycle check.** Sanity depends on Attention, Interaction and Suit; none of them reads any Sanity value except Interaction's gate query, which reads the SIM bit (never `PERCEPTION_OUT`). Attention reads nothing of Sanity (one-way, SN-7). There is no cycle in the simulation graph; the only loop in the design (Attention to sanity to lies to player noise to Attention) passes through the human.

## Tuning Knobs

Every value is a `[PLACEHOLDER]` registered as such unless the row says **cited** or **relation** (reflexion P-1); tuned in `assets/data/sanity/` (gameplay values are data-driven, never hardcoded). A ★ marks a **registry candidate**: a cross-system fact another GDD reads, gates on, or must stay consistent with. Unstarred knobs are Sanity-internal and are registered as placeholders but are not facts other systems cite. **Retune rule (P-2, P-7):** any change to a stage threshold, a harm or tell threshold, `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, an exposure rate, the divisor, or a floor re-runs D.4, D.8 and D.9 in full before it is accepted. The D.4 margins are thin (2.4 s on bound (i), 5.6 s on bound (iii)) and cap 36 is the largest passing value, so a knob that raises the descent rate or moves a threshold up is a knob that can fail a lead invariant. Safe ranges below are computed **with every other knob at its default**. Columns follow the template: too-high and too-low symptoms are what a tester sees, not abstractions.

**Tuning order for the first playtest.** (1) `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` and the three `AMBIENT_FLOOR` values decide whether class (b) appears in 30 minutes (hypothesis: stage 1 after about 5.3 min aboard in ring 1). (2) `ONSET_PM` and `LIES_MAX` then set intensity, measured by the Debug Overlay's `eligible_entries_per_min`. (3) The thresholds and the cap are touched last and only with D.4 re-run, because they carry the friendly-harm fairness. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: every safe range above is derived with the other knobs at their defaults from the boot assertions, and the boot sweep AC-SN-160 holds that derivation: each range is tested at both endpoints, which must boot, and one step outside, which must refuse.)*

## Visual/Audio Requirements

Sanity owns **no sprite sheets** and no sound assets. This section states WHAT must be seen and heard, for WHOM, and that every lie leaks; it does not specify shaders, easing curves, pixel widths the art bible has not fixed, or sound content (those are the Perception Filter's, the technical-artist's and the audio-director's). Every value quoted is the art bible's (cited) or a Sanity or registry value (cited). Where the bible fixes nothing, this section says so and routes the number to the Perception Filter GDD or the technical-artist lighting spike (ART 8.12) instead of inventing one. The art-director was consulted (row ids `SNV-n`, "Sanity Visual": `VA-n` is Suit's own prefix and `HR-n` the bible's). Tags: **MVP-live**; **MVP-conditional** (the bible calls for it, cost or scope confirmation owed to the producer and technical-artist); **DEFERRED(VS-A)**; **DEFERRED(VS-B)**. *(Perception Filter and Adaptive Audio & Tells are designed, `perception-filter.md` and `adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`; this section states what they must draw and play.)*

**Two things this GDD says plainly, so nobody promises too much.**

1. **MVP stage 2 is the own-cone rim fringe plus the lamp-housing glow sprite. It is not "light that lies".** The bible's stage-2 row (ART 4.4, 2.9) lists "cone appears to reach further", "shadows with no occluder", "violet-black shadows", "false reach tinted" and "apparent warmth". Every one of those except the fringe is a Lighting decoy kind (`GLOW_EXTENSION`, `PHANTOM_SHADOW`; LO-25, LO OQ 16) and is DEFERRED(VS-A) (`DECOY_OVERLAYS_MAX` 4, `DECOY_MAX_CH` 0.85, `DECOY_LEAK_PX` 2, `DECOY_LEAK_ALPHA_PM` 300, all cited and never restated). The only MVP "light lie" is the lamp-housing glow **sprite** (module-body channel, SN-16 reach): the real lamp brightness from `served_pm`, the floor light pool, `lux` and the beam radius stay true.
2. **There are two different violet fringes with two different causes; they share a look and nothing else.**

| | Own-cone rim fringe (stage 2 and up) | Crewmate-outline fringe (class c) |
|---|---|---|
| Seen by | the lying seat, on its OWN beam's rim | a viewer, on ANOTHER seat's outline and beam edges |
| Driven by | the viewer's own SIM `stage` being 2 or deeper AND a stage-2 reach lie being ACTIVE for that seat (lie-bound; the Filter's `RIM_MODE` AMBIENT, on whenever `stage >= 2`, is a playtest-gated opt-in) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | the subject seat's SIM `tell_on` bit (SHARED_SUMMARY); also the lie-leak on a monsterised rig (SNV-17) |
| Geometry | one continuous band tangent to the true cone rim, no root, never in a falsely extended reach | 2 art px outside the Void Steel contour, the full outer silhouette AND both edges of the beam cone (ART 5.5) |
| Motion | breathing 4 to 6 s, per viewer, must not lock to `tell_beat` | breathing 4 to 6 s (HR-7), never flicker |
| Visible to its owner? | yes, while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE: it is a leak of that lie and not a standing self-sign (SN-14, user ruling on OQ-SN-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | **no**: "a player never sees their own fringe" (ART 5.5) refers to this one only |
| Phase | MVP-live, lie-bound default (AMBIENT a playtest-gated opt-in) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | DEFERRED(VS-B) |

**Strands versus fringe (Attention's resolved contention, ATT:93, AC-AT-109).** Attention's `web_strand_*` are radial, discrete, rooted at a seam, locked to `tell_beat`, global, and exist only in the true lit region. Sanity's fringe is continuous, tangential, unrooted, breathing, per viewer, and never locks to `tell_beat`. **Hue-removed still-frame test (AC-SN-154):** with hue removed, a still frame of a cone rim with strands and one with the fringe must be told apart by geometry alone. If the fringe cannot be tangential, continuous, breathing and per-viewer, that is a CONFLICT for the creative-director (ATT:93).

**Stage-2 rim fringe: lie-bound (user ruling on OQ-SN-21, 2026-10-27).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The fringe is drawn only while a stage-2 reach lie is ACTIVE for the seat, so it is never a free reading of the level and it keeps the no-free-reading-of-the-level line of Game Feel; IA-39 ("no sanity meter or number exists") therefore never has to tolerate a de facto stage indicator. The cost, accepted by the ruling: SN-14's standing self-sign shrinks, so the actor's diegetic sign is the stage-1 gleam on instruments and the leaks of the lie itself. The state-bound alternative (on whenever `stage >= 2`; the art-director's earlier position, ART 4.4's standing leak, ART:73 "subtle is allowed, absent is not") survives only as the Filter's `RIM_MODE` AMBIENT, a playtest-gated opt-in, and would need the creative-director's reading of IA-39 before it could become a default. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the question was a USER DECISION taken before sprint planning, OQ-SN-21, with both consequences on the record; the design review recommended lie-bound, which the user adopted.)*

### Class (b), false system state (MVP-live)

Kinds by true state and stage reach come from SN-16. Reach ladder = intensity ladder: stage 1 needle, readout and inspect card (the same instrument as its wall gauge, AD-C1 resolved); stage 2 plus state glyph and lamp-housing glow sprite; stage 3 plus the whole machine sprite ("the lie extends from the gauge to the sprite", CON:246). Each lie carries the HR-5 tri-modal leak (violet gleam, glide motion, sound signature; each mode sufficient; at least two of three always present per L-L).

| ID | Lying surface | Tag | What is drawn lying | Leak | Binding rules |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNV-7 | Gauge needle (dial arc) | MVP-live | The needle departs the true angle at the **glide rate** (0.5 to 2 deg/s on a dial arc, ART 7.3) toward the comfort side (FALSE_OK, GLIDE on a degraded subject) or the alarm side (FALSE_FAIL, GLIDE on a healthy one), then holds the false region with a slow micro-wander (below 0.5 Hz) that never fully rests. | Hue: violet fringe `#B07BE6` on the glass or needle edge, never the core. Motion: the glide itself, the one motion no real instrument makes (slow, smooth, directional, uncaused, never at rest). Sound: a signature exists and begins no later than the glide (audio-director). | The needle never **snaps** to its false value (a jump is unsignalled change and borrows neither signature of 7.3). Honest motions stay honest: *settle* (damped spring 250 to 400 ms, overshoot at most 5 percent, then still) and *tremor* (plus or minus 1 art px, irregular, 6 to 10 Hz, returns to rest) carry no gleam and are never used by a lie. In-world gauges carry no numerals at game distance (3.1, 7.6 item 5): needle angle and zone band only. |
| SNV-8 | Digital readout and inspect card | MVP-live | Honest numerals **tick once per real change, never tween**. A lying readout ticks **continuously** as it tracks the glide, or **holds stale** while the ship audibly and visibly changes. Tick interval at least 334 ms, default `LIE_TICK_MS` 400 ms, `T_tick = max(LIE_TICK_MS, N_r x 334)` (registry: `lying_digit_tick_schedule`, `LIE_TICK_MS`; Perception Filter D.9; consecutive transitions at least 334 ms apart because 1000 / 333 = 3.003 exceeds `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3; the accessibility-specialist confirms, OQ-SN-23). | Gleam on the card glass; the continuous-tick or stale-hold is the motion mode; sound as SNV-7. | The inspect card is **the same instrument** as the wall gauge: it lies exactly when the gauge lies, with the same tells, from stage 1 (7.0, HR-14). The card frame stays an anchor (HR-6); violet appears only on the reading's glass, never the frame (4.6). The card is drawn only for a currently lit instrument. |
| SNV-9 | State glyph, lamp-housing glow sprite, whole machine sprite | MVP-live (stage-gated) | State glyph: the false state frame, static, with the BREATH motion mode (PF-15 static-pair row); a glyph never takes a digit tick *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: conforms to Perception Filter PF-15, BREATH)*. Lamp-housing glow sprite: the housing's emissive-APPEARANCE frame is swapped; **no `Light2D` is created or altered, the floor light pool and `lux` do not change**. Whole machine sprite (stage 3): SPRITE_SWAP shows one of the machine's own honest state frames: a rotor spinning on a stopped machine (the rotor cadence drifts and never settles to the honest rate), or a housing shown cracked on a whole one. | Gleam at the housing, glass or sprite EDGE (never a fill); motion: BREATH for glyph, glow sprite and a static machine frame (the gleam never stops breathing while ACTIVE); the rotor cadence drift for a continuous machine frame, which never settles *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; sound as SNV-7. | **Consequence-channel firewall (L-C):** a false-broken sprite adds no scorch, no sparks-as-arc-POP, no mist, no drip, no frost, no puddle, no hull hairlines. "Housing shown cracked" is the machine's own honest broken *frame* (a `mach_*` state frame), never a `trail_*` hairline asset, because integrity hairlines along ribs are a consequence-channel trail (3.3, 5.5). A glow-sprite lie is read by the player as exposure (Principle 3), so it must be an unlit overlay frame that cannot be mistaken for a lantern pool. Real running pipes are truth. |
| SNV-10 | Own-cone rim fringe | MVP-live, lie-bound (AMBIENT a playtest-gated opt-in; user ruling on OQ-SN-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | One continuous band tangent to the TRUE cone rim, no root, breathing 4 to 6 s (per viewer). Colour `#B07BE6`, edge-only, never a fill. | Not a lie; it is the stage-2 leak of a live reach lie (SN-14), drawn only while one is ACTIVE for the seat. | Discriminator and test above. Width: **the bible fixes none for the own-cone rim** (the 2 px of 5.5 is the crewmate outline); the Filter proposes it in the lighting spike under three constraints: it never extends the true reach, never occludes or alters rim pixels of the true beam (the true light radius is an anchor, Principle 1), and never locks to `tell_beat`. |
| SNV-11 | Lie end: EXPIRED, INVALIDATED, DEBUNKED | MVP-live | **Presentation-identical for all three end types** (the end type must not be an oracle; DEBUNKED must pass the design-test sentence: no verdict icon, card, line or cut to the truth). The needle's displayed value returns to the true value **on the glide path (at the Filter-owned return rate `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE` 3.0 deg/s, registry, not the 0.5 to 2 deg/s departure glide `GLIDE_RATE_BAND`), never a snap and never a settle** (AD-C2 resolved in SN-16). Leak amplitude is a function of the **displayed-minus-true discrepancy** (the gleam fades as the discrepancy closes), so the leak ends with the lie and no separate "lie over" event exists to read. Sprite swaps: OQ-SN-24. | A needle snapping back is uncaused motion; a *settle* would borrow the honest-change signature (7.3). After a DEBUNK the player learns the truth from the world (a hiss, a draught, a scanner **reading**), not from the interface saying so. | The glide rate is slow (0.5 to 2 deg/s), so a large dial discrepancy returns over tens of seconds during which the needle visibly disagrees after a paid scan; the Filter uses the separate, faster `GLIDE_RETURN_RATE`, still never a snap or a settle. |
| SNV-12 | `field_check` and scanner reading | MVP-live | The scanner shows an honest **reading** in the reading register (thin, light, moving marks inside a still frame, 3.4): never a verdict icon, never a real-or-fake binary, never Alarm Red or "safe green" (IA:972, F-3). No colour is "safe" in this game (4.2). | MVP scanner is an anchor (SN-19). VS-A lie-capable at stage 3 only, behind `SCANNER_LIE_STAGE`, carrying the full HR-5 tri-modal. | Truth (MVP) |
